C. RAY WHITAKER
Grand Master
October 10, 2001 – October 16, 2002

ii

C. RAY WHITAKER
Grand Master – 2001-2002
Our Grand Master, C. Ray Whitaker, is a native Californian. He
was born on October 18, 1931 in Madera, California, and has made his
home there ever since. He married his childhood sweetheart, Joyce, and
they have been married fifty years. They have two married sons, three
granddaughters and one grandson. He was educated in the Madera
County Schools, graduating from Madera High School in 1950. After
service in the Air Force during the Korean War, he received an honorable discharge and returned to Madera to take over the family business,
Whitaker’s Auto Wrecking, which had been started by his father in
1930.
Ray and Joyce are members of the First Christian Church in
Madera. He has served the “Madera Old-Timers Day” as a “vigilante
sheriff”, and for six years he served the local schools as a volunteer
crossing guard, and as an assistant for a reading teacher. He has also
been active in the Boy Scouts of America for over 45 years, including a
number of years as a commissioner. During that time he was an assistant scoutmaster, and Explorer advisor. Brother Ray has been honored
with one of Scouting’s highest awards – The Order of the Silver Beaver.
In addition he found time to support the Campfire Girls, the
International Order of Rainbow for Girls, and the International Order of
DeMolay. He served several terms as “Rainbow Dad” and as a
DeMolay advisor. He holds the DeMolay Legion of Honor.
He began his Masonic journey in Madera Lodge No. 280 in
1957, and served as Master five times: 1965, 1979, 1980, 1982 and
1997 of Madera Lodge, and of Joppa Lodge No. 508, which is consolidated with Madera Lodge No. 280. He served seven years as Inspector
of the old 412th Masonic District, and was Grand Sword Bearer for
Grand Master Roy J. Henville, Grand Marshal for Grand Master Sam
M. Pavlovich, and served four Grand Masters as Grand Tiler. In addition to serving on many Grand Lodge committees, such as the Youth
Orders and the Membership Development Committees, he has served as
a director for Nob Hill Masonic Management, and as a trustee for the
Masonic Homes of California, The California Masonic Memorial
Temple and the California Masonic Foundation.
He is a Thirty-Third Degree Inspector General Honorary in the
Scottish Rite, and served as the Personal Representative for the Fresno
iii

Scottish Rite. He is a York Rite Mason, and a member of the Tehran
Shrine Temple. His other Masonic memberships include the Sciots, The
National Sojourners, The Royal Order of Scotland, the Robert The
Bruce Association, Southern California Research Lodge, The Scottish
Rite Research Society, and Masonic Origins Research Lodge in Clovis.
In addition, he is a Past President of the Madera High Twelve Club –
twice! He and his wife Joyce are members of Madera Chapter, Order of
the Eastern Star, where he served as Worthy Patron in 1962 and 1965.
Brother Ray’s hobbies include traveling, woodworking and
country music.

PUBLIC OPENING CEREMONY
Sunday, October 13, 2002
2:00 PM
The Public Opening of Grand Lodge was held in the Masonic
Auditorium of the California Masonic Memorial Temple, 1111 California Street,
San Francisco, California beginning at 2:00 p.m. Grand Master C. Ray
Whitaker, accompanied by his Grand Lodge Officers, entered the Auditorium to
the sound of the organ, and assumed their stations. The Grand Chaplain gave the
invocation.
The Grand Master welcomed all in attendance to the Public Opening of
rd
the 153 Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of California. He thanked
Brother Michael D. Nella, Master of Madera Lodge No.280 for raising the
Grand Lodge.
The Grand Master then noted that the gavel used for the session was
hand crafted by Brother Ray Booth and presented to him at his Installation as
Grand Master on October 10, 2001 by his wife, Joyce, and their two sons Doug
and Tom.
The Grand Marshal was directed to retire to escort into the auditorium,
the leaders of the concordant and appendant bodies of the Family of
Freemasonry in California. The following were present:

To the youth leaders the Grand Master remarked, "We will have the
opportunity to hear (you) during our program today, but for now, I want to tell
each of you, how much you are appreciated for all the work you do on behalf of
your organizations." And to the adult leaders, he said, "It is a very special
pleasure for me as Grand Master to express the appreciation of the Grand Lodge
of California for your leadership of our Masonic Youth Orders. These young
people are the future of Masonry. To you, therefore, is committed a very special
responsibility on behalf of our Fraternity."
The Grand Master directed the Grand Marshal to conduct the adult
leaders of the Masonic Youth Groups to seats in the auditorium, and then to
conduct the youth leaders from California to seats in the East.
Throughout the remainder of the afternoon’s program, the Grand
Master called on each of the elected youth leaders in turn for their very special
remarks. Each speaker was well-received with applause from the audience.
Each elected youth leader presented a special gift to the Grand Master.
Visiting dignitaries from other Grand Jurisdictions were then conducted
into the auditorium, accompanied by the flag of his state or nation. The flags
were presented by the San Francisco Scottish Rite Knights of St. Andrew. The
Grand Marshal introduced the visiting Grand Masters, representatives of other
Grand Masters, and visitors from other jurisdictions:

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The Grand Master graciously welcomed the distinguished guests and on
behalf of the Masons of California expressed our appreciation for them having
joined us. The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to afford the guests
seats in the auditorium.
The Grand Master then directed the Grand Marshal to retire and present
the Flag of Our Country before the Altar of Freemasonry. The flag was
presented at the members of San Juan Chapter Order of DeMolay from the
Northern California DeMolay Jurisdiction..
The Grand Master then invited all citizens to join him in the Pledge of
Allegiance to the Flag of Our Country and the singing of “God Bless America”.
Speeches by Youth Order Leaders
During the afternoon program the Grand Master called upon each of the
four elected youth leaders for their remarks. They all delivered inspiring
messages to those assembled in the auditorium. As each youth leader favored us
with his or her remarks, the Grand Master presented each one with a check for
leadership training. Each youth leader in turn thanked the Grand Master and
presented him with a special gift.
Grand Master's Youth Support Award
The Grand Master then called upon Brother Raymond Daley, Chairman
of the Grand Lodge Youth Orders Committee to assist him in presenting the
Grand Master’s Youth Achievement Award to this year’s four honorees. The
award recognizes singular service to the young people in the Masonic Youth
Orders.
The Grand Master directed the Grand Marshal to escort the recipients
to a place West of the Altar. The Grand Master and the Deputy Grand Master
then presented the awards while Brother Daley read the biography of the
following honorees:
Worshipful James J. Nash, Palm Springs Lodge No. 693
Nominated by Mrs. Sara Lee Martin, Supreme Deputy
Grand Assembly of California, IORG
Brother James J. Nash was initiated into Pt. Firman Lodge, which later
consolidated with San Pedro Lodge and served as Master three times. He was
active with the Job's Daughters in Lomita and served on the Guardian Council
for 14 years as well as the DeMolay Chapter in San Pedro.
Bro. Nash received the Hiram Award in 1979, the first year that it was
given, in recognition of his service and dedication to the Masonic Order.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

9

After moving to Palm Springs with his wife Virginia, in 1991, Bro.
Nash demitted to Palm Springs Lodge, No. 693, served as Master twice and
remains involved today. In addition, he serves on the Youth Orders Committee.
After their move to Palm Springs, Bro. Nash became interested in
Rainbow and began supporting the Rainbow Girls in the desert area shortly
thereafter. He currently serves as Chairman of the Whispering Sands Assembly.
His wife Virginia is the Grand Deputy for both Desert Flower and Whispering
Sands Assemblies.
Brother Nash has worked as part of the Floor Crew at California Grand
Assembly for many years and is always available to do whatever is necessary in
any area. He has also been a faithful worker at the Southern California Rainbow
Summer Camp for the past nine years and assists the Camp Director wherever
help is required. He personally urges every girl from Whispering Sands to
attend Summer Camp and the girls love having him there. He has even been
known to take part as a "special entry" in the pantaloon parade.
There is no question that Bro. Nash is a first-class supporter of
Rainbow for Girls and worthy of the Grand Master's Youth Support Award for
2002.
Worshipful James A. Marshall, Mission Lodge No. 169
Nominated by Mrs. Karen Francis, Grand Guardian
International Order of Job’s Daughters
Bro. James A. Marshall is a member in good standing of Mission Lodge
No. 169 in San Francisco. He has served on the Youth Orders Committee of his
Lodge for many years and has worked tirelessly for Job's Daughters, Rainbow
for Girls and the Order of DeMolay.
From 1948 to 1970 he served on the Guardian Council of Bethel No.
107, San Francisco. His Council positions have included Associate Bethel
Guardian, Guardian Treasurer and Chairman of Fraternal Relations. Then from
1970-1975, he was requested to serve as Associate Bethel Guardian of Bethel
No. 198, Daly City. When that Bethel closed in 1975, Bro. Marshal returned to
Bethel No. 107, San Francisco, and has served all these years as Associate
Bethel Guardian. He was selected to be Grand Outer Guard in 2002, and he has
traveled the state visiting various fraternal organizations promoting the youth
groups. This accounts for 54 active years of service for the International Order
of Job's Daughters.
In addition to his service to Job's Daughters, Bro. Marshall has also
served as Rainbow Dad for San Francisco Assembly from 1977 to 1979. He was
Chapter Dad for Mission DeMolay, San Francisco, for 10 years, from 1974 to
1984. He has served our youth for many years; always willing to help, congenial
with everyone he meets, and is always focused on the youth. He is a good role

10

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

model for others to see how he cares for and promotes the youth of our Orders.
Brother James Marshall, recipient of the Grand Masters Youth Support
Award for 2002.
Worshipful Edwin D. Clarke, Los Altos Lodge No. 712
Nominated by the Youth Orders Committee
Grand Lodge F. & A. M. of California
Brother Edwin Clarke, a member in good standing for 50 years of John
A. Brashear Lodge, No. 743 in Dormont, Pennsylvania and a dual member of
Los Altos Lodge No. 712, has been active in both Lodges.
Brother Clarke began his service to the Masonic Youth Orders when he
was appointed to the Advisory Board for San Jose Chapter, Order of DeMolay,
and as a district supervisor in the Pacific DeMolay Association. In 1971,
Brother Clarke was appointed Administrative Officer for the Pacific DeMolay
Association, an office he held until 1981. From 1971 to 1985 Brother Clark
served on the staff of the DeMolay International Leadership Conference at
camps in California and Arizona. Brother Clarke was appointed Assistant
Executive Officer in 1981 and served until 1992 when he was appointed
Executive Officer for NorCal DeMolay, an office he presently holds.
Brother Clark is an active member of the International Supreme Council
of DeMolay and presently serves as Grand Standard Bearer.
Brother Clarke has also served as Bethel Guardian for San Mateo
Bethel, No. 181. A letter of support for his nomination for this Award from Mrs.
Ana Lind, Past Supreme Deputy in California, International Order Rainbow for
Girls, lauded Brother Clarke for his assistance and support for the Rainbow
Girls.
In all, Brother Clarke has served 39 devoted years for the California
Masonic Youth Orders, and continues to this day.
Well deserved, Brother Edwin D. Clarke, recipient of the Grand
Master's Youth Support Award for 2002.
The distinguished Masons were saluted with the Public Grand Honors.
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Marshal to conduct the recipients to
seats in the auditorium.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

11

Daniel Carter Beard Masonic Scouter Award
The Grand Master announced that the Grand Lodges of Masons in this
country, together with the Boy Scouts of America, recently created the Daniel
Carter Beard Masonic Scouter Award to recognize Master Masons who have
provided outstanding service to youth through Scouting. As Grand Master, he
authorized this award to be presented in California. Introducing the presentation
of the first awards he remarked:
There is no question that the relationship between the Grand
Lodge of California and the three Masonic youth organizations
has been and will continue to be a strong, positive and
mutually rewarding one. Personally, I have enjoyed my
association as an adult advisor for Rainbow and DeMolay.
Throughout my life I have also had the opportunity to work
with other youth organizations, including the Boy Scouts of
America. As you may know, one of the founders of Boy
Scouts was a Mason, Brother Daniel Carter Beard.
Brother Dan founded a male youth program in the late 1800's
called the Society of the Sons of Daniel Boone. By 1905, the
program had become "The Boy Pioneers." Lord Robert
Baden-Powell read of Beard's program, and based on his own
military experience, developed what is now known as the Boy
Scouts.
The new Boy Scout program came to America in 1910, when
Brother Dan merged his organization into the Boy Scouts of
America and became its first National Commissioner. Brother
Dan was raised a Master Mason in Mariners Lodge No. 67,
New York City, New York.
The Grand Master then introduced Mrs. Francena Butler, Golden Gate
District Executive who delivered remarks on behalf of the Boy Scouts of
America. Following her remarks, the Grand Master read the names of the
recipients and instructed the Grand Marshal to conduct them West of the Altar
where he and Mrs. Butler invested them with the certificate and medallion of the
award. The recipients were: Lester W. Miller, Thomas P. Emery, Michael C.
Kovalich, Donald G. Souza, Richard A. Garver, Richard H. Kleitman, Nicholas
S. Radcliffe and James E. Bonine. (Knowlton K. Shore, Perry W. Coy and
Gregory R. Gray were also recipients, but were unable to attend the Public
Opening.)

12

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Masonic Homes for Children in Covina
The Grand Master introduced Mr. John Howl, Executive Director of the
Masonic Homes of California Covina Campus and Mrs. Barbara Ten Broek,
Director of Children and Community Services in Covina. Mr. Howl expressed
his gratitude to all of the Masons and guests present for their continued support
of the children's program in Covina.
Following his remarks, a ten-minute video was shown that described the
children's program and remarkable impact it had on the lives of the children it
served. At the conclusion of the video, 46 of the children from the Home and
the adult family speicialists processed on to the stage where they met the Grand
Master and his wife, Joyce Whitaker, West of the Altar. The children presented
the Grand Master and his wife with a gift and a donation to their special project.

DEVOTIONAL SERVICE
The Grand Master then observed that as the program drew to a close,
we will turn our thoughts inward and to the attention of the Grand Chaplain for
an Inspirational Message.
The Grand Master then called upon the Grand Chaplain, the Very
Reverend Thomas M. Boles, for his message. [The Grand Chaplain’s message
appears in Appendix G.]
Worshipful Brother Donald A. Nielson then sang an inspirational
rendition of “The Power of One.”
The Grand Master thanked the Very Reverend Tom Boles for his truly
inspirational message and Worshipful Don Nielson for his excellent
performance.
The Grand Master concluded by thanking all for attending the Public
rd
Opening of the 153 Annual Communication of Grand Lodge. He led the Grand
Lodge Officers out of the auditorium.

MONDAY MORNING, OCTOBER 14, 2002
rd

The 153 Annual Communication of the Grand Lodge of Free and
Accepted Masons of the State of California was called to order by Grand Master
C. Ray Whitaker, on Monday, October 14, 2002.
Worshipful David E. Johnson, Secretary and a Past Master of Madera
Lodge No. 280 raised the opening session of Grand Lodge. The following gavels
were used: a California Grizzly Bear Gavel, presented on November 24, 2001,
th
at the 150 Anniversary Celebration of Mariposa Lodge No. 24; a gavel on a
wooden plaque, presented by Silver Gate Three Stars Lodge No. 296 at the
Installation of Officers on December 22, 2001, in San Diego.
The Grand Secretary was asked to call the roll of Grand Lodge
Officers, Past Grand Masters, and Past Grand Officers, and to ascertain if a
constitutional number was present to convene the Grand Lodge of California.
The following were present as indicated by their response to the calling of the
roll:
Elected Officers

Masons registered as of the end of the day, with at least twenty percent of the
Lodges chartered by this Grand Lodge represented, and that therefore a quorum
was present as required for the transaction of business.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The Grand Master then opened Grand Lodge in ample form. The
Grand Marshal and the Deacons retired to escort the Flag of the United States of
America into the Auditorium, which was followed by the Pledge of Allegiance.
The Grand Master then asked those assembled to sing one verse of “God Bless
America”.
Escort of the Past Grand Masters
The Grand Master then asked the Past Grand Masters to retire so that
they could be escorted into the auditorium and introduced. This being done, the
Grand Master welcomed the Past Grand Masters by expressing his appreciation
for the dedicated service that they have rendered to the Grand Lodge. The
Grand Marshal then introduced each Past Grand Master, following which the
Grand Master, accompanied by the Deputy Grand Master, personally greeted
each. They were then escorted to seats reserved for them in the auditorium.
The following were asked to rise as groups for recognition: Hiram
Award Recipients, Golden Veteran Award Recipients, 75-Year Recipients,
Worshipful Masters, Lodge Secretaries, Grand Lodge Committee members,
members of Constitutional Boards, District Inspectors, Former Grand Lodge
Officers, and Visiting Brethren from other jurisdictions. Each group was greeted
with a round of applause.
Memorial Service
The Grand Master called on Worshipful Allan L. Casalou, former
Grand Chaplain, and Chairman of the Memorial Service Committee, to present a
service of remembrance for our departed brethren.
Reports of Grand Lodge Officers, Boards and Committees
The Grand Master then called on selected Boards and Committees to
make brief reports to Grand Lodge during the Monday Morning session. The list
of reports made by Grand Lodge Officers, Boards, and Grand Lodge
Committees are included here for ease of reference. Where a motion is made by
an officer or committee chairman (as for the Trial Review Committee), the
motion is included below at the appropriate time in the schedule. The reports are
to be found in the Appendix as indicated after each Board or Committee.

Grand Master concluded by saying, " Our prayers are with him in this time of
difficulty for Brother Ralph, and for his beloved wife, Frances, as well."

Legislation
The Grand Master stated that the Grand Lodge would begin
consideration of the legislation before us by considering the Grand Master’s
Decisions and Recommendations. He said that the session would continue until
4:30 p.m., picking up on Tuesday where the Grand Lodge left off at the close of
the Monday afternoon session.
The Grand Master called upon Worshipful Brother John C. Nolan,
Chairman of the Jurisprudence Committee, to make his Report on the legislation
before the Grand Lodge. Brother Nolan moved that he be allowed to present an
emergent matter for consideration by Grand Lodge. The motion was seconded
by the Grand Lecturer, Very Worshipful Harold A. Macy. The Grand Master
called for the vote and the motion was passed.
Brother Nolan then moved that the version of Resolutions No. 02-02,
02-04, and 02-05 distributed to the delegates this morning be substituted for the
version of those resolutions mailed to the Lodges last July. The Grand Lecturer
seconded the motion. The Grand Master called for the vote and the motion was
passed.
The Grand Master called upon Worshipful Brother Donald Finton,
Chairman of the Finance Committee, for his report.
The Grand Master announced that the Grand Lodge was then going to
consider the Grand Master’s Decisions and Recommendations, the Carry-Over
Legislation from 2001, and the new legislation which was properly submitted for
consideration at the Annual Communication. He reviewed the rules for
conducting debate on legislation outlined in Part V of the Ordinances, in the
California Masonic Code.
Then the Grand Master asked the Deputy Grand Master to assume his
station, while he delivered to Grand Lodge the Grand Master’s Decisions and
Recommendations for their consideration.

TUESDAY MORNING, OCTOBER 15, 2002
Grand Lodge was called on promptly at 9:00 a.m. by Grand Master C.
Ray Whitaker and the Grand Chaplain offered prayer. The Grand Master
announced that Grand Lodge had been raised this morning by Worshipful
Brother Kevin Hall, Master of Palo Alto – Roller Lodge No. 346.
The Grand Master announced that this session was raised with the
following gavels: a walnut gavel handcrafted form the original beam from Ophir
Bear Mountain Lodge No. 33, and presented on the occasion of the Cornerstone
Dedication on April 27, 2002, at Murphys; a gavel and sounding block made by
th
Brother George McDonald, and presented on the 150 Anniversary celebration
of Madison Lodge No. 23 at Grass Valley on May 9, 2002; a gavel made from
wood which was originally a part of the “horse barn” at the Masonic Home at
Covina, and presented on June 28, 2002, at the Re-Dedication Ceremony for the
Home for Children; a wood gavel and sounding block with a carved Scottish
Rite emblem, presented by Brother Marquez, Venerable Master of the Ventura
Lodge of Perfection, at the New England Picnic at Ojai, California, on June 29,
2002; a gavel and sounding block made by Brother Bill Hodgkinson, and
th
constructed of African Zebra wood. It was presented at the 75 Anniversary
celebration of John D. Spreckels Lodge No. 657 on August 30, 2002, in San
Diego; a gavel made by Brother Richard Nyberg, and shaped like the “El
th
Camino Real Bell”. It was presented on August 31, 2002 at the 50 Anniversary
celebration of Oceanside Lodge No. 381; a handcrafted gavel by Brother Barney
th
Oldfield, presented on November 9, 2001, at Auburn at the 150 Anniversary
celebration of Eureka Lodge No. 16; a “Salvage Yard Gavel”, presented on
September 17, 2002, at the reception for the Grand Tiler, Wor. Brother Lloyd
Garbutt. It was made by Brother George A. Bass of Mosaic Lodge No. 218.
Regarding this gavel, the Grand Master remarked, "I cannot take the time to tell
you about the symbolism built into this gavel, but it has lots of Masonic
numbers. For example, there are three valves in it, and these represent,
according to Brother Bass, our Three Most Excellent Grand Masters;” and
finally, a "Traveling Gavel” from the Supreme Pyramid of the Ancient Egyptian
Order of Sciots that started in Texas, has been to the East Coast, and returned to
California.
The Grand Master then proceeded to consider the Carry-Over
Resolutions from 2001 and the 2002 Resolutions. The text of these items is to
be found in Appendix I, together with committee reports as given, and the vote
on each.
The Grand Master reiterated the rules governing the proper protocol
and manner of discussion on the various pieces of legislation.
At 12:00 Noon the Grand Master called Grand Lodge from labor to
refreshment, to be called on again at 1:00 p.m.

TUESDAY AFTERNOON, OCTOBER 15, 2002
At 1:00 p.m. the Grand Master called the Grand Lodge from
refreshment to labor, following which the Grand Chaplain offered prayer. The
Grand Master then announced that the Grand Lodge had been raised for the
afternoon session by Worshipful Richard Delsi, Master of Mt. Jackson Lodge
No. 295.
The gavels used were the following: a setting maul and sounding block
of Amaranth wood from Guyana in South America. The carrying box was made
from cedar wood from Tennessee. It was handcrafted by Worshipful W. M.
Richardson, Past Master, and Presented by Worshipful John Irwin, Master of
th
Acacia Grove Lodge No. 351 on the occasion of the 100 Anniversary
celebration of their lodge on August 3, 2002; a gavel handcrafted by Greg
Kirkpatrick, and presented by the Master of Palo Alto – Roller Lodge No. 346,
Worshipful Bro. Kevin Hall, on August 23, 2002, at their centennial. It is made
of ash wood – a twin tube, fluted at each end, and a sounding block.
The Grand Master called on the Grand Secretary for the final report of
the Credentials Committee, which showed that there were 318 Lodges registered
as being in attendance, and a total of 1,288 Master Masons and others (being
Masters of Lodges, Past Grand Masters and Grand Officers, and so on)
registered.
The Grand Master continued with the consideration of the Resolutions
properly before Grand Lodge. When all matters had been considered, the Grand
Master thanked the brethren for their attention during the deliberations. He
announced that the results of the voting would be available after the Installation
of Officers on Wednesday, October 16, 2002. The results would be mailed to the
Lodge Secretaries after the close of the Annual Communication.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Adoption of the 2002-2003 Grand Lodge Budget
The Grand Master called on the Deputy Grand Master, Right
Worshipful M. William Holsinger, to present the 2002-2003 Grand Lodge
Budget. He did so, and a copy of the budget is found in Appendix B. The
Chairman of the Finance Committee, Worshipful Donald W. Finton, moved the
adoption of the 2001-2002 Budget, which was seconded by the Grand Treasurer,
and was carried by a majority affirmative vote.
Adoption of the 2003-2004 Grand Lodge Per Capita
The Grand Master then called on the Senior Grand Warden, Right
Worshipful Howard D. Kirkpatrick to present the 2003-2004 Grand Lodge Per
Capita. He did so, and a copy of the proposal is found in Appendix C.
The Chairman of the Finance Committee, Worshipful Donald W.
Finton, moved the adoption of the 2002-2003 Grand Lodge Per Capita in the
amount of $22.15, which was seconded by the Grand Treasurer, and carried by a
majority affirmative vote.
Election of Officers
The Grand Master then asked the Grand Secretary to explain the
balloting procedure for the election of officers. No ballot would be necessary if
the office were not contested. If more than one nomination were received for an
office, ballot cards would be distributed for the purpose of balloting for that
office.
Election of the Grand Master
Deputy Grand Master M. William Holsinger was nominated Grand
Master by Most Worshipful Melville H. Nahin, Past Grand Master. No further
nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the nominations
be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot
for Right Worshipful M. William Holsinger to serve as Grand Master for the
ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was declared unanimously
elected.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

29

Election of Deputy Grand Master
Senior Grand Warden Howard D. Kirkpatrick was nominated Deputy
Grand Master by Most Worshipful Alvin J. Weis, Past Grand Master. No further
nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the nominations
be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot
for Right Worshipful Howard D. Kirkpatrick to serve as Deputy Grand Master
for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was declared
unanimously elected.
Election of Senior Grand Warden
Junior Grand Warden David R. Doan was nominated Senior Grand
Warden by Most Worshipful Allen B. Gresham, Past Grand Master. No further
nominations appearing, it was moved, seconded and carried that the nominations
be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot
for Right Worshipful David R. Doan to serve as Senior Grand Warden for the
ensuing Grand Lodge Year. Upon his acceptance, he was declared unanimously
elected.
Election of Junior Grand Warden
The Grand Master announced that pursuant to Section 510 of the
California Masonic Code, Worshipful Brother Frederick L. Sorsabal, a Past
Master of Anaheim-Azure Lodge No. 533 in Anaheim had been recommended
for the office of Junior Grand Warden of the Grand Lodge of California for the
ensuing Grand Lodge Year. The Grand Secretary then read the biography
previously circulated in accordance with this section, while Brother Sorsabal
stood before Grand Lodge. He was then escorted to the Grand Tiler’s Room,
while other nominations were called for. None appearing, the Junior Grand
Warden moved that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot
for Worshipful Brother Frederick L. Sorsabal to serve as Junior Grand Warden
for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year. The motion was seconded by the Grad
Lecturer and unanimously carried. Upon his return to the floor of the Grand
Lodge, and upon his acceptance, he was declared unanimously elected by the
Grand Master.
Election of Grand Treasurer, Grand Secretary, and Grand Lecturer
The Grand Master then announced that pursuant to Section 510 of the
California Masonic Code, the Executive Committee had recommended that the
following be nominated to serve for the ensuing Grand Lodge Year:

that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot for Warren J.
Blomseth to serve as Grand Treasurer. The motion was duly seconded and
adopted unanimously, and upon his acceptance, the Grand Master declared him
elected.
Election of Grand Secretary
Most Worshipful Stanley M. Cazneaux, Past Grand Master, nominated
Very Worshipful John L. Cooper III to serve as Grand Secretary for the ensuing
Grand Lodge Year. No further nominations appearing, he moved that the
nominations be closed, and that the Grand Treasurer be instructed to cast a
unanimous ballot for John L. Cooper III to serve as Grand Secretary. The
motion was duly seconded and adopted unanimously, and upon his acceptance,
the Grand Master declared him elected.
Election of Grand Lecturer
Most Worshipful Stanley L. Channon, Past Grand Master, nominated
Worshipful Harold A. Macy to serve as Grand Lecturer for the ensuing Grand
Lodge Year. No further nominations appearing, he moved that the nominations
be closed, and that the Grand Secretary be instructed to cast a unanimous ballot
for Harold A. Macy to serve as Grand Lecturer. The motion was duly seconded
and adopted unanimously, and upon his acceptance, the Grand Master declared
him elected.
Grand Lodge Called from Labor to Refreshment
The Grand Master then called Grand Lodge from labor to refreshment
for the purpose of installing the Grand Lodge Officers for the ensuing Grand
Lodge Year. The Installation was announced as being at 11:00 a.m. on
Wednesday, October 16, 2002.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

35

GRAND MASTER’S MESSAGE
I stand here before you today as I did a year ago, with a profound sense
of humility, gratitude and apprehension. No man can serve as your Grand
Master without feeling how small and insignificant he is, nor without the
generous help and support of all of you. The resultant humility is real; it’s
constant!
Your response and strength have been most effective and gratifying.
For this I will be ever grateful. The measure of this gratitude, I’m sorry to say,
cannot be expressed in words. Throughout the year I have tried to express my
gratitude in my actions and words, but the year has now come and gone, with
much left undone and unsaid. Now I stand before you to be accountable for my
actions taken this past year.
This year has been in the shadow of September 11 – a day burned into
the memory of each American. An appeal went out to our brethren and our
lodges to help those whose lives had been devastated by this act of terrorism, and
in particular, to help the families of the policemen and firemen who gave their
lives trying to help others. The appeal organized by the Grand Lodge of New
York to help these families resulted in the donation of more than $100,000 from
California to a fund that eventually was in the millions. Freemasonry in general,
and California Freemasonry in particular, can be very proud of the generosity
exhibited in a time of great need. Masonic relief was indeed exemplified at its
best, and I thank and everyone of you who gave so unselfishly to those in need.
Many knowledgeable Masons have indicated to me that we have among
our Masonic Fraternity in California the best brains of men anywhere in the
world, and if our membership and fortunes continue to fall, it may be necessary
to again summon these men to study our problems and offer solutions.
Section 1060 of the California Masonic Code requires the Grand
Master to “present, at each Annual Communication, a written message setting
forth: (1) All his official acts during the year; (2) The general condition of
Masonry within the jurisdiction, and (3) Recommending such legislation as he
deems necessary or expedient for the welfare of the Craft.” The last consists of
four Grand Master’s Recommendations which have already been distributed to
you, and upon which I will comment at the appropriate time that they are brought
before you for consideration. The first consists of my Grand Master’s Decisions,
of which there were five, and which will, in like manner, be explained when they
are placed before you for vote. In addition, the published report of this session
will list all my appointments for the year, and all the dispensations issued for the
better governance of the Craft. My Grand Master’s Itinerary will also be
published with the Proceedings of this Annual Communication, together with
lists of those who received the Golden Veteran’s Award and the Diamond
Veteran’s Award.

36

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

My remarks this morning will focus on the general condition of
Masonry in this jurisdiction, and to that I now invite your attention.
My theme as Grand Master was Communication: Key to Education. I
have tried to implement this theme in spirit as in letter as I have traveled the
length and breadth of this jurisdiction. Masonic Education – the commitment by
each Mason to a lifetime of learning how to apply the teachings of Freemasonry
to his life – was my principal concern. But without an effort to improve our
ability to communicate the teachings of Freemasonry our Masonic Education
must fall short. I therefore devoted much time and effort to encouraging Masons
to make daily progress in Freemasonry. Through organized programs of
Masonic Education, through leadership training programs, and through personal
contact, I worked to communicate to our members the real meaning of seeking
“further Light in Masonry.”
A highlight of our Masonic Education effort this year was the Second
Annual California Masonic Symposium, held in Sacramento the last weekend in
July. With almost 150 registered participants, it was the largest gathering for
Masonic Education in recent history. The list of speakers was impressive, the
topics were thought-provoking, and the fellowship was warm. Due to a personal
commitment I could not be present, but appointed our Deputy Grand Master,
Right Worshipful Bro. Bill Holsinger, to represent me. It was jointly sponsored
by the Grand Lodge Masonic Education Committee, and the Institute for
Masonic Studies. Bro. Bill Wine chairs the Masonic Education Committee, and
Bro. Joel Springer is the Director of the Institute for Masonic Studies. Together
they organized an outstanding event. It was our pleasure to make Bro. Brent
Morris – a leading Masonic scholar of our generation – a Fellow of the Institute
for Masonic Studies at the concluding banquet. This event certainly was a
culmination of my Grand Master’s Theme: Communication – Key to Education.
At the beginning of my year as Grand Master I called together the
committee chairmen for two workshops – one in Northern California, and one in
Southern California. I gave them specific goals and objectives for the year in
harmony with our efforts to improve Grand Lodge programs. Their response
was heartening, and I am pleased to report to you that many of the committees
contributed to the improvement of our Grand Lodge programs this year. It is
difficult to single out just one committee, but the Temple Properties and
Taxation Committee took on new responsibilities, and was energetically led by
Bro. Tom McKinney. As a result they helped lodges and hall associations to
accomplish their goals with success, and the Grand Master and Executive
Committee were much better informed than ever before on the state of our real
estate holdings in this jurisdiction.
Financial integrity of Grand Lodge is one of the main responsibilities of
the Grand Master. This year – for the first time – we implemented a “zero-based
budget” which thoroughly analyzed the costs of each program, and helped your
Grand Lodge leadership make appropriate decisions for the allocation of scarce

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

37

dollars. The Deputy Grand Master will present next year’s budget at a later
time, and the Senior Grand Warden will present his budget, together with the per
capita needed to fund it. You can be assured that their recommendations are
based on a sound analysis of our financial base, and on the most careful
allocation of your money. We will end the year in the black, despite a continued
decline in membership, and the financial reports clearly show that your per
capita was spent wisely and responsibly.
As Grand Master I continued the long range planning process begun
under Most Worshipful Melville H. Nahin in 1999. The Research and Planning
Committee met four times during the year, and provided thoroughly researched
information to help your Grand Lodge make good decisions for the future. You
will hear a report on that process from the Chairman, Bro. Larry Adamson, at a
later time in this session.
In order to be a successful organization we must have good leaders. It
is perhaps in the area of leadership training that my theme, Communication –
Key to Education came into its own more than any other. The following is a
brief report on our various programs.
The Wardens’ Leadership Retreats continued to have a positive effect
on our lodge leadership. Although only half of our lodges sent their Wardens,
those that did were rewarded with well-planned and informative programs. The
anonymous evaluation forms completed after each session indicated that we had
met our objectives – and exceeded them – in the eyes of the participants. This is
the only leadership training program in which the wives of our lodge officers are
involved, and their involvement is one of the reasons why the program is so
successful. Lodges that make the families of their members welcome are lodges
that are vibrant examples of applied Freemasonry. The Wardens Retreats show
our top officers how to involve our wives in the Lodge, and how to make family
members feel at home in the Masonic environment.
I cannot leave this section without paying tribute to Bro. Fred Sorsabal,
and his wife Marlene. Fred served as Chairman of the Wardens’ Leadership
Retreats for three years, and his leadership brought the program to new heights.
He and Marlene were a great example of a leadership team, and together they
were responsible for the continued success of this important program. You will
hear more from Fred, of course, because he is the Recommendee for election this
year as Junior Grand Warden.
I presided at two workshops for our Inspectors, one in Northern
California, and one in Southern California, last November. Inspectors serve as
the representative of the Grand Master to our lodges, and it is important that they
have the necessary skills and information to properly discharge those important
responsibilities. Almost all of our Inspectors attended, and the results are
evident in their ability to be of greater service to your Grand Lodge, and of
greater help to our lodges.

38

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Another outstanding program of leadership training was that headed by
Bro. Rick Hutchinson, and his team of trainers, with the Officers’ Management
Workshops. Designed to help the junior officers of the Lodge become proficient
in the programs and laws of Grand Lodge, they were popular programs, and
well-attended. They are also our longest running program, beginning in
November each year, and concluding in August. Appreciation is expressed to
Rick and his team for a fine job well done!
Our Grand Secretary continued his series of workshops for our
secretaries – six of them this year. Secretaries are the Chief Administrative
Officers of our Lodges, and a capable Secretary is essential to the success of any
Lodge. He was joined by Bro. Joel Springer, our Assistant Grand Secretary, and
the attendance this year by Secretaries, and others, was impressive.
It is easy to forget that membership is the lifeblood of any organization,
including Freemasonry. Because we may not ask a man to become a Mason it is
easy to forget that there are thousands of men who would make good Masons if
they only knew something about Freemasonry. There are actually several
programs dealing with membership about which I would like to report.
The Public Relations Program begun in 1999 under Most Worshipful
Melville H. Nahin continued, with refinements and improvements. In particular,
the following should be noted.
This year I laid 30 cornerstones, of which 25 were for public schools or
public buildings of some sort. These opportunities to showcase Freemasonry at
the local level were not lost. Partnering with the local lodge, our Grand Lodge
Office staff sent out press releases and press kits in advance, and – in some cases
– placed paid advertisements in local newspapers. We also followed up with the
local press, and the result was some excellent coverage about Freemasonry in the
local community.
In addition to the opportunities offered by our cornerstone laying
ceremonies, we worked with local lodges at the Los Angeles County Fair, the
San Diego County Fair, and the California State Fair. In doing so we were able
to bring professional help to assist the local effort to provide Masonic
information to the public. When joined with the very popular Child I.D.
Program, these local events make Freemasonry visible in a way that could not
have been possible without a coordinated effort from Grand Lodge. I want to
pay particular tribute to Bro. Allan Casalou, the Executive Director for Grand
Lodge, who headed up our Public Relations effort, and produced excellent
results for Grand Lodge, and for our lodges.
We had a Membership Development Committee whose main thrust was
to handle inquires from our two websites, and direct them to a local
committeeman for follow-through, or to a lodge directly when no committeeman
was available. We have not kept statistics to tell us how many of these inquiries
eventually filled out an application for the degrees of Masonry, but we believe

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

39

that it was substantial. Younger men obtain their primary information from the
Internet, and this program enabled us to meet them, inform them about
Freemasonry, and encourage them to consider applying. Bro. Paul McCleary
headed up this effort for us this year.
In passing, I want to pay a special tribute to three men who helped
make my theme, Communication – Key to Education a reality in cyberspace.
First, Bro. Dave Stites, a Past Master of Lakeside Lodge No. 760 in San Diego.
Dave was the first webmaster for our newly formed members website,
freemason.org, and then became its technical and operations chief. He also
developed the website provided to our lodges – Calodges.org, and managed that
program very successfully. He was assisted by Bro. Norm Schultz, the
webmaster for freemason.org since 1999, and by Bro. Brad Bradbury, the “online” editor for the electronic version of the California Freemason magazine.
Each in his own way contributed to the growth of Freemasonry in California by
helping us communicate with our members as well as with the public.
You have to ask a Mason to become a Mason. We all know that. And
yet it is curious that in an organization where you have to ask to become a
Mason – for you will never be asked by another Mason to join – we pay very
little attention to membership attrition. We are doing something about
membership attrition, although not enough. Suspension for non-payment of dues
continues at much too high a level. The following programs address the issue of
member retention.
In March I sent out a survey to all Entered Apprentices who had failed
to advance. I asked them why they had not continued with their Masonic degrees
and become Master Masons. The responses were enlightening, and I will share a
few with you:
•

68% told us that they want to advance, but were prevented from doing
so for a variety of reasons. Only 15% told us that they were not
interested in advancing.

•

Of those who told us that they were interested in advancing, the biggest
reason cited for not advancing was a lack of time – primarily a lack of
time because of work.

The issue of lack of time for candidates to learn the work and to
advance must be addressed. Although we have shortened our proficiencies a few
years ago, that apparently did not solve the problem of time – at least as
perceived by a large number of our candidates who fail to advance because they
believe that they do not have the time to learn the work. Whether or not the
“one-day class” is a solution for this remains to be seen, but it is obvious that
something must be done if we are solve the problem.
Two years ago we adopted a Basic Education Program for all our

40

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

candidates. Although required by our law, far too few lodges give this program
the priority that it deserves. When properly implemented it can have a very
positive impact on keeping new members. After all, if a man learns nothing
from his experience of having been made a Mason, it is our fault – not his. If he
resigns, or is suspended for non-payment of dues, it is our fault – not his. If we
fail to interest him in Freemasonry we have only ourselves to blame. The
Masonic Basic Education Program is simple, but essential, to a candidate
learning about Freemasonry at his most formative stage. The impact that we
make on a new Entered Apprentice Mason can make all the difference as to
whether he stays or leaves.
Grand Lodge also has an optional “Mentoring Program” available for
our Lodges to use. While it is optional, those lodges who use it find that new
members become strong and committed Masons. A mentor is assigned before a
man receives his First Degree, and continues to work along side him as he makes
his progress toward becoming a Master Mason, and afterward. A mentor
becomes a friend as well as a teacher. A mentor teaches by example as well as
by instruction. And a mentor has a lifelong impact on his student. If all our
lodges had an active Mentoring Program I am convinced that our membership
problems would evaporate.
I was particularly proud this year of the support the Craft gave to Joyce
and me for a new scholarship we established, with the permission and
encouragement of the California Masonic Foundation. Most scholarships go to
students who have been successful academically in high school – those with the
top grades, who find learning easy. There are students, however, for whom
learning is much more difficult. Usually as bright as the best of the students,
they may suffer from a disability that prevents them from learning in the
traditional fashion, and as a result, do not earn the top grades. Dyslexia, or a
problem with learning to decode words, is but one example. These students
cannot compete for college scholarships when a main criterion is a grade point
average. Thanks to the generous support of Masons and their families and
friends throughout California, we will shortly be able to establish a scholarship
fund for students who have learning difficulties. As of today, more than $50,000
has been contributed to the California Masonic Foundation for this new
scholarship! My gratitude for your support will be echoed down the years by
students who benefit from a college education that might otherwise have been
denied to them. Thank you on their behalf!
Communication: Key to Education was my theme this year as Grand
Master, and I cannot leave this part of my message without paying tribute to
perhaps the greatest Masonic “communicator” of this, or any generation. I refer
to Bro. Ralph Head, who for twenty-nine years was Editor of the California
Freemason magazine. Ralph resigned this last May for reasons of health, and he
is in our thoughts and prayers as he copes with his illness. His career in
Masonry began in 1935 when he was Raised to the Sublime Degree of Master

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

41

Mason. He was a leader in this Fraternity for more years than most of us have
been Master Masons. Ralph served as a trustee of the Masonic Homes of
California for an unprecedented 25 years, and was President three times; he was
President of the Los Angeles Masonic Service Bureau; he was Grand Treasurer
of this Grand Lodge for three years, and in 1985 was honored as Mason of the
Year by Most Worshipful Roy J. Henville. And he was our conscience and our
mentor as Editor of the California Freemason magazine – one of the best
Masonic magazines in the nation.
In 1989 Bro. Ralph wrote these words in an editorial in the California
Freemason magazine, and they are worth quoting here:
In the new era Masonry should inspire all Masons to live a Masonic
life. How a Mason lives outside the lodge is much more important than
what he does in it. Taking another degree, another oath, learning a new
sign do not necessarily evidence forward steps in Masonic living. We
need not change our rituals. What we need is a change in the
understanding of them. We need not change precepts or doctrines or
duties. What we need is to recognize their significance and how to
apply them in practical and useful ways in our daily lives.
What more could anyone add to those words? Only this: That Ralph
understood more than anyone else I know what it means to be a Mason. We
won’t forget that, Bro. Ralph. Not now – or ever!
Before concluding I want to pay tribute to our Grand Marshal, Bro.
LeRoy Foote, and his wife, Jeanne, for their devotion to our Grand Lodge, and to
Joyce and me, this year. Their thoughtful efforts during the year made our task
much easier, and are greatly appreciated. I also owe a great deal of thanks to our
faithful, supportive Grand Lodge Officers who have been outstanding
representatives of the Lodges of this jurisdiction. I also extend my thanks to the
inspectors, chairmen and committeemen who work diligently for our Craft. I
especially owe a great debt of gratitude to our Executive Committee, and to our
Grand Secretary, John Cooper, who works twenty-four hours a day for the Grand
Lodge of California. Thank you!
I also owe my entire being to God who watches over me twenty four
hours a day, three hundred and sixty five days a year, a this verse of Scripture
from Philippians, chapter four, verse thirteen: “I can do all things through Christ
who strengtheneth me.”
Last, but not least, I cannot close without thanking the best half of my
life – Joyce – who has stood by my side through fifty-one years of marriage,
putting up with the good times and with the hard times. She is my constant
inspiration, companion and friend, and without her I would be nothing, as she is
my life. To her my heart-felt thanks go for allowing me to take this journey, and

42

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

to share with me those past years of service to our Grand Lodge. To her I will
be eternally grateful.
Thank you, brethren, for the privilege of serving you as Grand Master.
May the Spirit of the Living, Loving Grand Master be with us and inspire us all!
Respectfully submitted,
C. Ray Whitaker, Grand Master

APPENDIX B

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

65

REPORT OF THE DEPUTY GRAND MASTER
2002-2003 Grand Lodge Budget
M. William Holsinger, Deputy Grand Master
(As mailed to the Secretaries of the Lodges in a
Memorandum dated August 26, 2002)
Section 7040 of the California Masonic Code requires the Deputy
Grand Master to prepare the Budget for the year he will serve as Grand Master,
beginning November 1 and continuing through the following October. The
Executive Committee has been working on this budget for many weeks. We
recently presented it to the Grand Lodge Finance Committee, and I am now
sending you the attached proposed budget for the 2002-2003 Grand Lodge year.
I ask you to review it with your Lodge, and your representatives who will be
attending the Annual Communication that they may come prepared to vote on
the budget.
The budget is balance utilizing as its principal source of revenue the
$20.91 per capita approved at the last Annual Communication. Applied to an
assessable membership of 79,193 it generates $1,655,926. Adding other revenue
makes total revenue $1,821,976.
This is the first time the “Corporate Services Budget” item is reflected
in the budget. This is a more precise method of charging the other Grand Lodge
entities (Homes, Foundation, CMMT and Nob Hill) for services rendered to
them by the Grand Lodge.
The Officer Travel allowance has been reduced, Accidental Death
Group Insurance carried on Grand Lodge officers has been dropped, and the
premiums for Property and Liability Insurance and Workers Compensation
Insurance which Grand Lodge provides for the constituent Lodges increased by
25% and will likely increase again next year – a direct result of the September
11, 2001 disaster.
The budgeted expenses for the year are $1,753,299, leaving a surplus of
$30,320 to be allocated to the Grand Lodge Endowment Fund.
As our membership continues its decline, it is increasingly obvious that
each Grand Master must continue his commitment to reducing expenditures, and
the membership will be asked to support an increase in per capita to offset
inflationary costs.

INSTALLATION ADDRESS

70

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

M. WILLIAM HOLSINGER
Grand Master of Masons in California, 2002-2003
Most Worshipful Past Grand Masters, visiting dignitaries, my fellow
Grand Lodge Officers, Brethren and Friends: I welcome you all to this Annual
Installation of the officers of this - your Grand Lodge.
It is an honor and a privilege to serve our great Fraternity; and I pledge
to you that I will do everything in my power to uphold the great traditions of this
Grand Lodge, and at the same time examine our strengths and weaknesses in an
effort to renew the vigor which at times we seem to have lost.
Our theme "Freemasonry Seeks Good Men and Makes Them Better Tell a Friend" should tell you that Masonic Education and membership
development will be the hallmarks of this coming year.
Membership development, while adhering to our high standards for
membership, is essential because if we continue our decline of some 6% per
year, the end result is quite obvious. We have a rich heritage given to us by our
ancestors - men who were devoted to our Fraternity - men who saw Masonry
flourish because of their efforts. Are we to be content watching their efforts
dwindle away only to be forgotten like some other organizations who have faded
from the scene? I think not, but it will require the effort of every one of us to
reverse this trend.
We all know men who would be a credit to our Fraternity - reach out
and invite them to attend an information night in your Lodge. You will be glad
you did, and so will they.
Our rosters contain the names of literally hundreds of Entered
Apprentice Masons who have not progressed beyond that First Degree of
Masonry, and sadly they have been such, in many cases, for years. Our Jr. Past
Grand Master sent a letter to these men early this year, and many responded
indicating a desire to return to their Lodges and complete their degrees.
In an effort to assist them, and with the cooperation of Al Malaikah
Shrine in Los Angeles, on November 23, 2002, the Grand Lodge Officers will
occupy the stations during a special meeting of Evergreen Lodge No. 259, and
will confer the First Degree of Masonry on a single candidate. All Masons, and
in particular Entered Apprentice Masons are invited to attend. The Degree will
be followed by a Masonic Education lecture by Wor. Robert Winterton of San
Diego. It is our hope that this experience will motivate the Entered Apprentice
Masons to return to their Lodges and become active Master Masons in their
respective Lodges.
While we are inviting men to look us over, we must at the same time
increase our efforts to impart more Masonic information to our members. Our

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

71

ritual is beautiful, meaningful, and full of guidelines on living a moral life, but
we must expand that effort; and we will be stressing the need for further light in
Masonry this year.
Another element of Masonic Education is education of our leaders - our
Masters and Wardens of the constituent Lodges. We have developed in recent
years the Officers Management Workshops for appointed officers and all
interested Masons; as well as the Wardens Leadership Retreats. I cannot stress
too much the benefits to be realized by our Lodges from sending men to these
leadership training sessions! Only 50% of our Lodges are taking advantage of
these programs. If your Lodge is one of the 50% not participating - I beg you to
re-examine your priorities. If you think your Lodge can't afford to send
representatives, I say you can't afford not to send them.
You will also be hearing much about our two wonderful Homes at
Covina and Union City. We know there are many widows, as well as some of our
own members, who do not know of the help and assistance that is available to
them, both on a resident and a non-resident basis. We intend to spread that word
through our talks, The California Freemason, and utilizing a new quarterly
newsletter that will be mailed to the leadership of each Lodge, the first being in
November.
I have frequently heard the comment emanating from our Lodges in the
northern part of this jurisdiction "the only time we hear from Grand Lodge is
when we receive our statement for per capita". Well, we are going to try to
change that this year. On June 10, 2003, nine of the Grand Lodge officers and
their wives will board a bus at Union City and travel north along highway 101,
visiting at least one Lodge each day, and delivering a message of what Grand
Lodge is doing, how we can help our constituent lodges, and what they can do to
help themselves. On reaching the Oregon border we will travel south through the
Sacramento River valley visiting Lodges along the way. This is referred to as the
"Masonic Information Tour", and that is what we plan to make of it.
I want to pay tribute this morning to three men who have made a deep and
lasting impression on me; and are responsible for me standing here today. First,
my Father, who was a member of Phoenix Lodge No. 178, now San Bernardino
Lodge No. 178. He was proud to be a Mason; and I know he is looking down on
us here today with great pride.
Secondly, a Past Master of Phoenix Lodge in San Bernardino, Wor.
Forest Keck. He was my high school counselor some 54 years ago, and today
resides across the bay in Oakland. In paying tribute to him, I honor teachers
everywhere. Teachers mold our young people, and do their best to create useful
citizens who will carry on the traditions of our great land - that is what Wor.
Forest Keck did for me.
The third person I want to honor is a Past Grand Master of this Grand
Lodge. I have had the opportunity to serve with eight different Grand Masters,

72

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

and I have gained from each of them. But the one who started me down this road
by appointing me Grand Marshal was Most Wor. William H. Price, Grand
Master in 1973. We lost Bill last year, but I know he is standing with my Father
saying "good luck Little Bill".
During the past twenty years I have presented numerous Masonic
Information Night talks - talks designed to acquaint those who are not Masons
with what Masonry is, and what it stands for. I have always concluded these talks
with a poem entitled "The Real Mason", and I would like to conclude my
remarks here today with that same poem - The Real Mason:
My Brother, Masonry means much more
Than just the wearing of a pin
Or carrying a paid-up dues receipt
So the Lodge will let you in.
You may wear an emblem on your coat,
From your finger flash a ring,
But if you're not sincere at heart
This doesn't mean a thing.
It's merely an outward sign to show
The world that you belong
To this great Fraternal Brotherhood
That teaches right from wrong.
What really counts lies buried deep
Within the human breast,
Till Masonic teaching brings it out
And puts it to the test.
If you can practice out of the Lodge
The things you learn within
Be just and upright to yourself
And to our fellowmen;
Console a brother when he's sick
Or assist him when in need,
Without a thought of personal reward

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

For any act or deed.
Walk and act in such a way
That the world without can see,
That none but the best can meet the test
Laid down by Masonry.
Always live up to your trust
And do the best you can,
Then you can proudly tell the world
You're a Mason and -a Man.

77

REPORT OF THE SENIOR GRAND WARDEN
Per Capita Recommendation for 2003-2004
Howard D. Kirkpatrick, Senior Grand Warden
(As mailed to the Secretaries of the Lodges in a
Memorandum dated August 26, 2002)
It has been a custom for the Senior Grand Warden to recommend at
Grand Lodge an amount for the per capita to be applied during the year he is
Grand Master.
This per capita income is the main source of income to fund all of the
many programs of your Grand Lodge. During the last two years we have been
developing budgets reaching out as far as five years. This gives us an early look
at future income and expenses.
I have been working with staff and the Executive Committee to cut
expenses, eliminate some programs, redesign the committee structure and keep
necessary increases to a bare minimum. The tragedy in New York had an
alarming effect on insurance rates this year, both liability and workers
compensation.
Even with these changes we are keeping; the budget increase to less
than 1% but it does require an increase of the per capita to $22.15. At the end of
the fiscal year in 2004 we will have a balanced budget with no excess and no
deficit.
I recommend to you a per capita of $22.15 for the fiscal year
2003/2004.

81

REPORT OF THE GRAND TREASURER
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
The Grand Treasurer is charged with the responsibility of reporting to
the members of this Grand Lodge the financial condition of the Grand Lodge and
its entities.
This past year has been a difficult one for our several entities resulting
from the events of nine-eleven of 2001, recent corporate executive scandals,
accounting scandals, the potential military conflict with Iraq, coupled with
economic uncertainty, have resulted in an adjustment in securities market values
not seen since the great depression of 1929 to 1932. Their emotional and
financial impact has been experienced at every level of your Grand Lodge. the
Nob Hill Masonic Management, Inc. had program cancellations which affected
their operating budget, but with determination and prudent management the
Board of Directors under the guidance of Warren J. Blomseth, President have
managed to stay within their established budget. However, some planned
projects were necessarily delayed or extended as a result of the financial
shortfalls. The California Masonic Memorial Temple Board and its President J.
Lloyd Clayton have completed several important improvement projects to the
Temple this year and are to be commended for executing their plans with great
fiduciary responsibility. Due to economic circumstances beyond their control it
made it essential to consider every project on its individual merit and immediate
need before proceeding and they have accomplished their goals admirably.
The California Masonic Foundation is approaching their financial needs
with the utmost due diligence in order to meet their continuing scholarship
requirements. Budgetary adjustments were made to accomplish the desired
result. The MSAP program, in order to obtain its financial objective, has
reduced the number of classes to be conducted over the next year. These were
difficult decisions for President Donald A. Nielson and the Trustees and they are
to be congratulated for the professional manner by which their mutual
obligations were accomplished.
The Masonic Homes Board President Frederick L. Sorsabal and his
Trustees have done an admirable job in staying on budget this year, while
continuing to achieve many of the planned objectives to the mutual benefit of our
residents. We know not what the financial future may be, but it is essential for
our mutual success in this endeavor, to exercise the greatest discipline and
profound wisdom to live within our established budgets to meet our infinite
responsibilities to our residents. I have no doubt that the present and future
Boards, will in consideration of a covenant established in the beginning with our
residents, continue to meet the fiduciary requirements needed for their
achievement. The bond of fraternal love and friendship should forever prevail
among our brothers and widows.

82

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

The Investment Committee and its Chairman Russ Charvonia are
responsible for not only the management of the Homes and Endowment Funds
but the several Foundation Trusts, to include the pooled income and retirement
funds. They accomplished their goals with the aid of their consultant and
professional money managers. Considering the volatile and uncertain financial
markets over the past year your Investment Committee has done an exemplary
job in the performance of their duties in comparison to other foundations and
endowments.
The reports and comments of the Grand Treasurer are based on the
official examination and statements of our certified public accountants whose
reports are an integral part of the Grand Treasurer’s Report. Their audits cover
the years most recently ended and contain both a complete statement of assets
under management as well as a record of the income and expenses for the period.
Fiscal responsibility is the cornerstone of our fraternity and you can rest
assured that the assets and investments of your Masonic entities are managed in a
manner of which you can be justifiably proud.
I am pleased to report that the Grand Lodge and its related entities are
in a sound financial condition. You should however remember that the size and
extent of our investments, and more importantly our charitable activities, were
financially possible in large part through the generosity of prior generations. If
we are to ensure the future of these fine programs, we must continue our
Masonic tradition of financial support. In this regard you have recently received
our new philanthropy solicitation from our new Director of Philanthropy Doug
Ismael, for your continuous charitable consideration and responsibility.
Remember, the delegates to the Annual Communication are in effect, the
stockholders of the entities of Grand Lodge and you are encouraged to read and
review this report. Your observations and comments are appreciated.
The past two years serving as Grand Treasurer have been an enjoyable
and educational experience, one that I will truly treasure all the days of my
remaining life. My special thanks to you my brethren for electing me to this high
office and a special thank you to our Grand Master, The Most Worshipful C.
Ray Whitaker, the Executive Committee, and the Grand Lodge Officers for their
support and cooperation this past year. To our Grand Secretary John L. Cooper
III, our Executive Director, Allan L. Casalou, and our former Chief Financial
Officers Douglas A. Scott and John Aldridge, and the accounting staff without
whom I could not have fulfilled the many responsibilities of this office, I offer
my deepest gratitude for their guidance, counsel, wisdom and support. Thank
you each of you for your sincere personal effort, your dedication and due
diligence in assisting me to have a successful year.
Respectfully submitted,
Joseph W.R. Ashwill, Grand Treasurer

163

REPORT OF THE GRAND SECRETARY
This is my eleventh report to you as Grand Secretary. The complete
report will be printed in the Proceedings of this Annual Communication, but here
are some of the important highlights of this past year:
Efforts continued to provide greater service to our lodges at lesser cost.
Two major projects furthered this goal. First, we implemented the new
Administrative Website, now used by more than half of our lodges. It
is also used by boards a committees, and now by the Masters and
Wardens of our lodges. It gives access to needed information from the
Membership Database, as well as materials in electronic format. It is
obvious that the leadership of our fraternity uses the website with great
frequency, and it has helped us to use our scarce funds much more
productively. For example, it used to cost your Grand Lodge more than
$15,000 a year to print and mail the Grand Master’s monthly itinerary.
Now very few copies are printed. The Itinerary is available for
download through the Internet from our website, and the cost is
minimal.
We also published the Proceedings last year in electronic format on a
“CD”. Some fifty lodges chose to subscribe to a printed version – at
their own cost – but most lodges chose to receive the “CD” copy, which
was free. We estimate that more than $18,000 was saved by this
change – money that could be much more effectively used to provide
greater services to lodges and others through the Grand Lodge Office.
The Grand Lodge Public Relations Program continued to assist our
lodges in promoting Freemasonry in connection with public events,
such as cornerstone laying ceremonies, and the Child I.D. Program. As
a result more lodges had an opportunity to become visible in their local
communities with the assistance of professional public relations
materials.
The Office of Philanthropy was established within the Grand Lodge
Office to coordinate our fund raising activities. We welcomed aboard a
skilled professional philanthropy officer, and the positive results are
already been seen. For the first time in the history of the Craft in
California, we had a coordinated campaign rather than a fragmented
effort. The new program saw the creation of an annual campaign to
replace the Masonic Homes Endowment fund effort of previous years.
The latter is now the target of a planned giving program, and promises
to provide long-term sustained funding for our charities. The annual
campaign this year focused on the needs of our Masonic Widows who
are served by the Masonic Outreach Program, and the Masonic Student
Assistance Program of the Foundation.
A new computerized registration system was implemented at this

164

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Annual Communication, considerably speeding up the process. In
addition, the new program of encouraging individual advance
registration significantly boosted the numbers of those attending our
events, and increased the advance purchase of tickets.
As usual, the printed report from the Grand Secretary’s Office will
contain reports and statistics about many items pertaining to our membership and
our lodges. As of June 30 of this year we had 82,318 Master Masons on our
rolls. We lost 5,315 Master Masons to death, withdrawals, suspensions for nonpayment of dues, and expulsion. We raised 934 new Master Masons, and
Initiated 1,191 Entered Apprentice Masons. We had 373 lodges on our roster as
of June 30 – seven less than a year ago.
It is my responsibility at this time to place before Grand Lodge the
names of four other Grand Lodges which are being recommended for
recognition by our Grand Lodge. The are as follows:
The Grand Lodge of Cameroon
The Grand Lodge of Djibouti
The Prince Hall Grand Lodge of Washington
The Prince Hall Grand Lodge of Pennsylvania
Both the Grand Lodge of Washington and the Grand Lodge of
Pennsylvania recognize their respective Prince Hall Grand Lodges, and we have
the consent of the former to recognize the latter.
Most Worshipful, I move that these four Grand Lodges be recognized
by the Grand Lodge of California.
Finally, my appreciation goes to our Grand Master, Most Worshipful C.
Ray Whitaker, and his corps of officers, with whom we have worked so well this
year. His commitment to the principles of our great fraternity inspired us to
greater achievements, and it was a privilege to have served him, and all of you as
well.
Respectfully submitted,
John L. Cooper III, Grand Secretary
