This Recommendation is consistent with Grand Lodge policy and
general purposes.
Recommendation ADOPTED.

Grand Master Whitaker’s Recommendation No. 4
Changing Name of California Masonic Memorial Temple To
California Masonic Memorial Center
Although Freemasonry is most emphatically not a religion, nor a
substitute for a religion, some of the terms used by Freemasons are construed by
the public to imply that we are. The use of our traditional language within our
tiled Lodges and a tiled Grand Lodge poses no problem; the use of some of our
traditional language on our public buildings does. In particular, the use of the
word “temple” on our buildings leads to the wrong impression on the part of the
public that our buildings are used for some time of “worship” in competition
with religious organizations. That is certainly not the case, but it is hard to make
this clear when we use the word “temple” on our buildings.
In recent years we have asked our Lodges to discontinue the use of the
word “temple” to describe their buildings, returning to the use of the much older
term “Masonic hall” or using the more modern term “Masonic center.” If this is
a good recommendation for our Lodges, it is also a good recommendation for
Grand Lodge. I therefore propose that the California Masonic Memorial Temple
be renamed the California Masonic Memorial Center. This change has the
unanimous support of the Board of Trustees of the California Masonic Memorial
Temple, and indeed, we have been informally using the term California Masonic
Memorial Center in many of our publications over the past several years without
any negative effect.
I therefore recommend that the California Masonic Memorial Temple
be renamed the California Masonic Memorial Center.
If you adopt this recommendation, the California Masonic Code will be
amended as marked:

Supervise, subject to the ultimate control of the Trustees of the

CALIFORNIA MASONIC MEMORIAL TEMPLE CENTER
§10010. PURPOSE.
The non-profit corporation, known as the California Masonic Memorial
Temple Center, shall hold title to and operate the California Masonic
Memorial Temple Center and all property owned by it.

§10020. COMPOSITION OF BOARD.
The Trustees of the California Masonic Memorial Temple Center shall
be nine in number. The Junior Grand Warden shall be a Trustee exofficio. The other eight Trustees shall be appointed by the Grand
Master for terms of three years each. The terms of at least two of the
appointed Trustees shall expire at the close of each Annual
Communication of Grand Lodge. When a vacancy occurs, the
appointee shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term. The Grand
Master may remove a Trustee at any time, upon request of two-thirds of
the Trustees, and appoint another.
If a member shall have served for nine consecutive years, including
service as a Chairman, he shall be ineligible for reappointment until the
lapse of one year.
§10030. MANAGEMENT.
The Trustees of the California Masonic Memorial Temple Center shall
elect officers within forty-five days after the close of each Annual
Communication of Grand Lodge. The Grand Secretary shall be
secretary and the Grand Treasurer shall be treasurer of the Board of
Trustees. They shall adopt, alter or amend By-laws for their own
guidance and for the management of the corporation, all subject to
approval of the Grand Master. They shall have the right to incur
indebtedness, but not to borrow money without the consent of the
Grand Master. The Grand Master shall at all times have the right of
visitation and inspection, and the supervision of the entire management.
§13040. NATIONAL MEMORIAL AND MEMORIAL TEMPLE
CENTER CONTRIBUTIONS.
In addition to the contribution to the Masonic Homes of California,
each applicant for the degrees shall accompany the application with a
contribution of $5 for the Endowment Fund of the George Washington
Masonic National Memorial, and $9 for the California Masonic
Memorial Temple Center. All such contributions shall be transmitted
by the Lodge to the Grand Secretary at the time and in the manner
specified on forms provided for that purpose. The Grand Secretary
shall pay the same over to the Grand Treasurer to be held subject to
allocation and payment by order of the Grand Master. Such
contributions are non-refundable.

Grand Master Whitaker’s Recommendation No. 5
Composition of the California Masonic Foundation Board of Trustees
At present the Board of Trustees of the California Masonic Foundation
consists of fourteen trustees appointed by the Grand Master for a term of three
years each. In addition, the Deputy Grand Master also serves as an ex officio
member of the Board, for a total of fifteen members. It is my recommendation that
the board be reduced to eight members, with the ninth member being the Deputy
Grand Master serving ex officio. The reduction in the size of the board by six
members will result in a cost savings to the Foundation, without any loss of
efficiency or control. The Board of Trustees of the Masonic Homes of California
has much more responsibility, and consists of only twelve members. Reducing the
Foundation to a total of nine trustees is logical.
If you adopt this recommendation, the California Masonic Code will be
amended as marked:
§11020. COMPOSITION OF BOARD.
The Board of Trustees of the California Masonic Foundation shall be
15 9 in number. The Deputy Grand Master shall be a Trustee
ex-officio. The other 14 8 Trustees shall be appointed by the Grand
Master for terms not to exceed three years each. The terms of at least
four two of the appointed Trustees shall expire at the close of each
Annual Communication of Grand Lodge. When a vacancy occurs, the
appointee shall serve the remainder of the unexpired term. The Grand
Master may remove an appointed Trustee at any time upon request of
two-thirds of the Trustees and appoint another. In appointing Trustees

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

241

the Grand Master shall give consideration to geographical
diversification in order that the entire Jurisdiction may have
representation.
If a Trustee has served nine full consecutive years, he shall be ineligible
for reappointment until the lapse of one year.
In addition, if you adopt this resolution, you will thereby grant the Grand Master
the power to adjust and/or terminate the terms of existing trustees to
accommodate the intent of this resolution.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
For several years, this Grand Lodge has been honing and fine tuning
various boards and committees. Grand Master Whitaker’s Recommendation
Number 5 seeks to reduce the number of appointed Trustees of the California
Masonic Foundation from 14 to 8. This reduction would result in a cost savings
to this Grand Lodge and would not affect the efficiency or control of the Board
of Trustees.
This Recommendation is consistent with the policy and general
purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Whitaker’s Recommendation No. 6
Conferral of Degrees in This Jurisdiction by Another Jurisdiction
As presently written, our California Masonic Code does not permit a
California candidate to receive his degree when an authorized Lodge or a degree
team comes to California from another jurisdiction. The degree team from another
jurisdiction must bring a candidate from the other jurisdiction rather than use a
candidate from California.
It is therefore my recommendation that a new Section 26385 be added to
the California Masonic Code which will allow a California candidate to receive his
degree when an authorized Lodge or a degree team from another Jurisdiction
confers a degree in a California Lodge using the ritual from the other Jurisdiction.
If you adopt this recommendation, a new section will be added to the
California Masonic Code as follows:
§26385. CONFERRAL OF DEGREES IN THIS JURISDICTION
BY ANOTHER JURISDICTION.

242

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

After consultation with the Grand Lecturer and the Ritual Committee,
the Grand Master may permit a Lodge in this Jurisdiction to invite a
Lodge or a degree team from another Jurisdiction to confer a degree
upon a candidate from the inviting Lodge, or from the invited
Jurisdiction, using the ritual of such other Jurisdiction. An elected
candidate of a Lodge in this Jurisdiction may receive a degree under
this section as long as the requirements of Section 26350 of this Code
are followed, and an Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft of a Lodge in
this Jurisdiction may receive a degree under this section as long as the
requirements of Section 26360 of this Code are followed. A candidate
from another Jurisdiction may receive a degree under this section as
long as the requirements of Section 26380 of this Code are followed.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The current California Masonic Code does not permit an authorized
Lodge or degree team from another jurisdiction to confer a degree on a
California candidate.
As presently written, the Code requires that a degree team from another
jurisdiction must bring a candidate from its own jurisdiction.
Adoption of this Recommendation will create a new section, Section
26385. This will permit an authorized Lodge or team from another jurisdiction
to confer that jurisdiction’s degree on a California candidate in a California
Lodge.
Any degree conferred pursuant to this section must meet the
requirement of Sections 26350 and 26360 and must have the consent of the
Grand Master.
This Recommendation promotes harmony and fellowship with other
jurisdictions. Thus, it is consistent with Grand Lodge policy and general
purposes.
Recommendation ADOPTED.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

CARRY-OVER FROM 2001 ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
Grand Master Decker’s Recommendation No. 1
Receiving Applications At Age 18
From time to time in recent years Grand Lodge has considered, and
then turned down, changing the age at which an application can be received for
the degrees of Masonry in this jurisdiction from age 21 to age 18. I believe that
this question needs to be considered once more, and therefore I am
recommending that Section 26010 of the California Masonic Code be amended
to state that an applicant for the degrees of Masonry must be a man who has
attained the age of 18 years of age, rather than the present age of 21. I ask you
to listen carefully to my reasons, and if you agree, to vote for Grand Master’s
Recommendation No. 1.
Freemasonry, unlike almost all other membership organizations, does
not recruit members. Each man must come to Freemasonry of his own free will
and accord. There is no exception. And we will not initiate a young man in his
non-age, nor an old man in his dotage. In both these cases the reason is the
same: a man must be capable of understanding the Art before be can become a
Mason. Each Lodge must determine whether a man’s advancing years have
rendered him incapable of understanding the degrees of Masonry. But there is
an absolute age below which we will not make a man a Mason. That age is now
age 21. It should be age 18, the age in America when a man enters fully upon
his manhood in every legal sense. Limiting the age to apply for the degrees of
Masonry to age 21 may have made sense when that was the age of majority. It
does not make sense now when the age of majority is age 18 in this country.
Today a man may legally sign a binding contract at age 18, may register to vote
at age 18, and may get married without parental consent at age 18. It is time that
Freemasonry changed its law to recognize this new reality.
It has been said by some that a man is not mature enough to understand
Freemasonry until he is at least 21 years of age. But I would ask, which of you
fully understood Freemasonry when you became an Entered Apprentice Mason?
It has been said by some that men younger than age 21 are incapable of
shouldering the responsibilities of being a Mason. But I would ask you to look
at those young men who go off to defend their country at age 18, shouldering an
enormous burden for their country, and who fight and die to keep us free. There
is hardly a burden greater than that. And it has been said that young men less
than age 21 are not capable of assuming the financial burdens that Freemasonry
requires. Again, I ask you to look around at the affluence of young men in this
age group who have responsible jobs, cars, and in some cases, are married and
have families. In many cases they are more capable that we are of carrying out
the responsibilities that Masons have.
But these arguments are not the main reason why I believe that we need
to make this change. Young men between the ages of 18 and 21 are at one of the

244

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

most formative periods in their lives. This is the time that they are making the
choices that will determine the quality of life that they will lead as adults. This is
the time that they need, more than at any other time in their lives, the moral and
ethical principles which Freemasonry can offer them. At a time in which we as
Masons are concerned that society has lost its moral compass, we presently deny
these young men the benefits of the teachings of Freemasonry which could shape
their lives in ways that no other experience could do. In operative times, Entered
Apprentices came to the Fraternity at age 14. They usually became Fellows-ofthe-Craft at age 18 or 19, and perhaps became Masters in their late 20’s. It was
a process that began at a young age, and reached its culmination over ten or
twelve years. But now we are Speculative Masons, and a man becomes a Mason
by being Initiated, Passed and Raised, all within the space of a year or less. That
process can happen as logically at age 18 as it can at age 21 – or at age 30, or
age 40, or at age 50, for that matter. A man is made a Mason when he is capable
of understanding the Art, and is ready to assume its responsibilities of his own
free will and accord. For the young man at age 18 who is ready to do so, we
must be ready to receive him.
Finally, our Grand Lodge is the last one on the West Coast to retain the
age of 21 as the age of admission. Alaska, Washington, Oregon, Nevada and
Arizona, all have age 18 as the age of admission into our Fraternity. In addition,
there are more than 20 jurisdictions in this country in which age 18 is the age of
admission to Freemasonry. There is nothing wrong with our young men in
California to make them any different. If our sons and grandsons lived in those
states, they could become Masons. Not so in California, and there is no good
reason why they should not become Masons here as well.
I look for the day when a young soldier who goes off to defend his
country takes with him the principles that Freemasonry has taught him. I look
for the day when a young college student who goes off to university takes with
him the teachings of our great Fraternity as the rule and guide of his conduct. I
look for the day when a young father, with a responsible job and a family, can
also be a Mason in California, even though he is not yet age 21. For too long we
have said “no” to young men who want to become Masons, and now we need to
say “yes”. I ask you to vote for this important change in our law.
The following sets forth the content of Section 26010, if my
recommendation is accepted:
§26010. MORAL AND PHYSICAL QUALIFICATIONS.
An applicant for the degrees of Masonry must be:
A.

A man who has attained the age of 21 18 years;

B.

A believer in a Supreme Being and a future existence;

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

C.

Of good moral conduct; and

D.

Without physical or mental impairment that would prevent him
from learning and practicing the principles of Masonry or
which would create a burden on the Lodge.

245

The question as to the physical or mental acceptability of the applicant
shall be left to the sound discretion of the Lodge. The Master shall
state the nature and extent of any such impairment upon receiving the
application and before the ballot is taken.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
In his Recommendation No. 1, Grand Master Decker sought to change
the minimum age in Masonry in California from 21 years to 18 years. Most
Worshipful Decker cited several reasons for making this change in our law.
First, the age of 18 has become the generally accepted age of majority in the
community at large. Next, the claim that a man has to be 21 to be mature enough
to understand the lessons of Freemasonry is arbitrary. Certainly maturity is an
individual attribute and the process of appreciating Masonic teachings is a
gradual one that continues over many years. However, the Grand Master’s
principle rationale for advocating a change in the age limitation is that it would
provide an opportunity for a person who is still in the formative period of his life
to be exposed to more important moral standards that will facilitate his entrance
into adulthood. In passing, he noted that California is the only jurisdiction on
the West Coast that has not made this change.
On past occasions, this Grand Lodge has had the opportunity to change
the minimum age of membership from 21 to 18. In the past, one concern has
been that this change would have serious impact on the leadership of DeMolay.
It is noteworthy that, when this legislation last came to the floor of Grand Lodge
in 1999, our Youth Orders Committee supported it. While that Resolution No.
99-31 was carried over to 2000, and received 59% affirmative vote, it failed to
muster the necessary two-thirds.
The other major area of concern has been that an 18 year old is not
mature enough to understand the message of Masonry. This argument was
discussed in depth by Grand Master Decker last year and will not be repeated
now.
This Recommendation will resolve an inconsistency that exists in
California. We are obligated to recognize 18 to 20 year old Masons from other
jurisdictions, but we do not allow California men of the same age to become
Masons.
There is nothing inherently biological or sociological about the age of
21 in terms of a man’s ability to carry out the duties of a Mason. Today we have

246

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

18 year-olds defending our Flag throughout the world. Accordingly, reducing
the age limitation is consistent with our general purposes.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Decker’s Recommendation No. 3
Refund Of Fees For Failure To Advance
In 1997 Grand Lodge approved Resolution No. 96-05, a carry-over
from the previous year, which restored the fees previously forfeited by Entered
Apprentices and Fellow Crafts, for failure to advance. That legislation was
designed to encourage Entered Apprentices and Fellow Crafts to advance by
relieving them of the burden of again having to pay the fees that they had
previously paid, but which had been forfeited. At the time this legislation was
adopted one other section of the code should have been changed as well, but was
not. I am recommending that we correct this error.
Section 26100 of the California Masonic Code requires that fees which
have not been forfeited for failure to advance must be refunded to the widow of
an Entered Apprentice or a Fellow Craft, or to the estate, in the event of the
death of the Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft. With the restoration of all the
previously forfeited fees, we inadvertently created an enormous liability for our
lodges. Lodges must carry on their books the fees paid by Entered Apprentices
and Fellow Crafts forever. In many cases these Entered Apprentices or Fellow
Crafts have disappeared because they lost interest, and the Lodge has no way of
contacting their heirs to discharge this obligation. That is an unreasonable
burden on our lodges. I am therefore recommending the Section 26100 be
amended to stipulate that after seven years these fees are no longer owed to the
heirs. If the Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft ever comes back, even after
many years, his fees are still paid. But if the Lodge has not heard from him in
seven years, there is no obligation to refund those fees.
This recommendation is made in the interest of fairness and equity for
our lodges, and I urge your support of Grand Master’s Recommendation No. 3.
If this recommendation is approved, Section 26100 will read as follows:
§26100. FEES FOR DEGREES
A Lodge shall not:
A.

Confer a degree without charge or donate a fee back to an
applicant under any pretext whatever;

B.

Confer the three degrees for a fee less than $50; or

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

C.

247

Confer the degrees for less than $75 where Lodges have
concurrent jurisdiction, except by consent of all such Lodges.

If the applicant has received the Entered Apprentice degree, or the
Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft degrees in another Lodge, the fee
for the remaining degrees or degree shall be that portion of the fee for
the three degrees as the By-laws of the Lodge shall prescribe.
If the applicant is elected to membership in another Lodge in this or
another Jurisdiction, under a Waiver of Jurisdiction or Dimit issued by
his Lodge, or dies before receiving a degree, the Lodge, if such
information comes to its attention within seven years after the applicant
was initially elected to receive degrees, shall return to the applicant or
to the legal representative of the applicant the sum fixed in the By-laws
for the degree or degrees not conferred, if the fees have not been
forfeited.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Decker recommended a time limitation of seven years on
a Lodge’s obligation to refund fees for Entered Apprentices and Fellowcrafts
who die or dimit within that period of time. This Recommendation is a logical
correction to California Masonic Code Section 26100. This Section currently
requires a Lodge to carry the refund liability for these fees in perpetuity, thereby
creating undue hardship for the Lodge.
This Recommendation is consistent with Grand Lodge policy and
general purposes.
Recommendation ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 01-05
Requires Only One Volume Of The Sacred Law On The Altar At A Time
The proponents of this Resolution seek to eliminate the requirement that a Bible
be on the altar where a candidate has chosen another volume of the Sacred Law
upon which to be obligated.

248

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 26295 of the California Masonic Code provides
for the use of alternative Holy Writings to be on the Altar when a candidate
desires that the Volume of the Sacred Law particular to his faith be used; and
WHEREAS, sub-section “E” of Section 26295 of the California
Masonic Code requires that a closed Holy Bible must remain on the Altar when
an alternate book is used; and
WHEREAS, this regulation implies that the Holy Writings pertaining to
the candidate’s particular religious faith are less important than a candidate
whose faith uses the Holy Bible.
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that Section 26295 of the
California Masonic Code be amended to read as marked:
§26295. ALTERNATE HOLY WRITINGS.
A candidate for the degrees in Masonry may select alternate Holy
Writings on which he will be obligated, but only under the following
circumstances:
A.

If a man does not wish to be obligated on the Holy Bible, he
must select alternate Holy Writings from a list promulgated from
time to time by the Grand Master of the Holy Writings of those
recognized religions whose theology is not inconsistent with a
belief in a Supreme Being and a future existence. The Grand
Master's list shall at all times include the al-Kitab al-Aqdas of
Bahaism, the Tripitaka of Buddhism, the Analects of
Confucianism, the Vedas of Hinduism, the Koran of Islam, the
Tanach of Judaism, the Koji-ki of Shintoism, the Adi Granth of
Sikhism, the Tao-te Ching of Taoism and the Zend Avesta of
Zoroastrianism. In selecting alternate Holy Writings, the man
must state that the book chosen is the Holy Writings of his
religious faith.

B.

The particular book selected for use on the altar during the
ceremonies of a man's degrees must be of a suitable size as
determined by the Ritual Committee.

C.

Grand Lodge shall make available all books on the list. Grand
Lodge shall loan any book on the list upon request to a Lodge
which requires it for a degree.

D.

The book selected shall remain on the altar throughout the
meeting during which it is used for a man's degree, unless the
Lodge schedules more than one man to receive a degree at a

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

249

meeting and all men receiving a degree on that occasion will not
be obligated on the same book of Holy Writings. In that event,
the book shall be changed when the Lodge is at refreshment
between the degrees.
E.

When alternate Holy Writings are used during a degree, a closed
Holy Bible of any size must be on the altar.

F.E.

Alternate Holy Writings may be used only at a Lodge meeting
during which a man selecting the alternate Holy Writings
receives a degree and on no other occasion.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Resolution addresses the issue of whether the Holy Bible must
remain on the altar when alternative Holy Writings are used during a degree.
As Freemasonry unites men of every country, sect and opinion, it is
appropriate that the obligation taken during each degree be made upon the
particular Holy Writings of a candidate’s faith.
Using only the volume of Sacred Law chosen by the candidate is
consistent with the principles and general purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

2002 RESOLUTIONS
Resolution 02-01
Alternative Dues Structure
The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit Lodges to automatically
increase dues when the Grand Lodge per capita is increased.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the California Masonic Code requires that each Lodge in
California adopt the Uniform By-Laws in Section 19040, which includes only
one method to set the dues of a member; and
WHEREAS, for the last several years, many Masters and Wardens have
asked for the ability of their lodges to adopt an alternative method to set the
annual dues; and
WHEREAS, the use of a formula to determine the dues of a member
does not violate any Masonic Tenet, there is no reason why a lodge should not
be able to adopt a formula to set its dues; and
WHEREAS, only one of the methods to set the dues of a member can
be included in a Lodge’s By-Laws, and, if this Resolution is adopted and a
Lodge desires to use the formula, rather than a singular dues amount, it need
only follow the procedures in Article XIII of the Uniform By-Laws to do so.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Article IX of Section
19040, Uniform By-Laws, of the California Masonic Code be amended as
follows:
ARTICLE IX
DUES
Section 1. The dues of each member of this Lodge shall be $_____
per annum, payable in advance on the first day of January.
Or in the alternative
The dues of each member of this Lodge shall be the sum of $______
plus the Per Capita sum approved by Grand Lodge at the immediately
preceding Annual Communication, per annum, payable in advance on
the first day of January.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

251

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to establish a method by which
dues of a Lodge would automatically increase or decrease based on the Grand
Lodge per capita assessment. It would establish an optional method for
determining the dues for a Lodge and the Lodge would choose one of the two
methods.
A Lodge could adopt (a) a fixed amount for dues as now required or (b)
a fixed amount for the dues of the Lodge plus the per capita assessment
approved by Grand Lodge. If a Lodge chooses to adopt the alternative dues
structure, it would only have to follow the procedures in Article III of the
Uniform By-Laws to do so. Life memberships would not be affected by this
change.
In 1998, a Resolution that mandated Lodges to change their dues
structure so that the per capita assessment automatically raised or lowered the
dues was defeated. This Resolution differs from that Resolution in that each
Lodge has the option to use one or the other method, giving each Lodge
flexibility in managing its dues structure.
This Resolution is consistent with our Grand Lodge policy and general
purposes.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
While this Resolution will have no financial impact on this Grand
Lodge, the Finance Committee feels that it will have a very important impact on
our member lodges.
This Committee also strongly feels that the financial stability of our
Grand Lodge is heavily reliant upon the financial stability of our local lodges.
The Finance Committee has been struggling with the necessity of raising per
capita assessment each year and at the same time finding some way to permit our
local lodges a means of recovering that increased assessment without having to
constantly amend their by-laws in order to increase their dues.
This Resolution permits, not requires, but permits, such action by our
lodges.
While the Finance Committee does not normally make
recommendations but only reports on the financial impact of a particular
resolution, we feel so strong about the necessity of this Resolution, that the
Finance Committee recommends a “yes” vote on Resolution No. 02-01.
Resolution CARRIED-OVER.

Resolution No. 02-02
Limits on Lodge Support for Charities
The proponents of this Resolution seek to clarify the specific use of the Lodge
fund and to promote the universality of the Fraternity.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, a Lodge in this jurisdiction is utilizing the Lodge fund to
support Christian Foundation for Children and Aging – Support of Catholic
Mission Worldwide – due to uncertainty of the Code; and
WHEREAS, that the Masonic Lodges consist of brethren with different
religious faith and ethnic background; and
WHEREAS, that the Lodge will not be able to support every existing
foundations and charitable programs in the world; and
WHEREAS, that if such contribution will be left under the discretion of
the Lodge, it will create disharmony among the brethren.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that California Masonic
Code Section 21060 be amended to read as follows:
§21060. USE OF FUNDS.
Lodge funds, including fees, dues, rental, profit or otherwise, are trust
funds set apart for the payment of the necessary, discretionary and
social expenses of the Lodge and for the special calls for charity. They
may not be invested in a life membership fund. Where funds are
donated and accepted in trust for any proper Masonic purpose they may
be used only for it. They may not be expended for alcoholic beverages,
Gifts or bequests to a Lodge, which are not limited to specific purposes,
may be used by the Lodge for any purpose permitted by this Code.
support any religious activities, or donations to foreign charitable
programs.
A Lodge or Temple Association shall not use any of its funds and
property to subsidize, directly, or indirectly, any organization using or
occupying its property, except Masonic Youth Orders. A Lodge may,
by approval of a majority of those members present and voting at a
Stated Meeting, allocate funds for community improvement, charitable
activity, or sponsorship of programs that will benefit residents of the
community in which the Lodge is located. A Lodge or Temple
Association may permit use of its facility without rental charge for
community or public activities consistent with the provision of this

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

253

Code, provided the user defrays any separate expense resulting from
such use.
Funds collected by an officer, committee, or member of a Lodge from
members of the Lodge for social purposes of the Lodge or its members
are received in trust as special funds and are not funds of a Lodge, and
can only be used for the purpose for which the funds were collected.
Receipts and disbursements thereof should not be entered in the Lodge
books, or deposited in or withdrawn from the Lodge bank account. No
officer, committee, or member of a Lodge shall use any pressure in
collecting such funds and all payments and contributions must be
voluntary. The Lodge shall approve a procedure for receiving
contributions, making disbursements, presenting accountings for such
trust funds, and disposing of surplus. Surplus may be designated for an
object or purpose which will be to the benefit or welfare of the Lodge
and may accumulate for one year after which time it shall become part
of the funds of the Lodge. All funds shall be subject to audit annually
and more frequently as the Lodge may direct.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
“To relieve distress is a duty incumbent on all men, especially on
Masons.” This is the philosophy upon which our great fraternity has been built,
and which still persuades men of good will and intentions to cross our portal.
Masons in our country contribute almost $2.5 million each day, or almost a
billion dollars a year, to our Masonic sponsored charities. We are enabled to aid
and assist our distressed worthy brother Masons, their widows and orphans- and
we do it well - and with love. When special needs such as earthquakes,
hurricanes and the terrible consequences of September 11, 2001 may require our
assistance, our Grand Masters have authorized and encouraged us to do a little
more.
But we of the Grand Lodge and our constituent Lodges cannot support
each and every worthy cause that may exist within our jurisdiction or without.
We must impose certain guidelines. These guidelines are presently set forth in
our California Masonic Code Section 21060.
Presently funds donated and accepted in trust may be used for the
purposes for which the funds are donated, providing they are “for any proper
Masonic purpose.”
According to this Resolution, the funds are not to be used to support
any religious activity or to donate to any foreign charitable program. The
proponents suggest that in adding this restriction, we would serve the cause of
promoting harmony within our fraternity by not permitting donations to specific
religious charities. The proponents argue that since Masonic Lodges consist of

254

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

brethren of different faiths and ethnic backgrounds, to permit such gifts would
create disharmony among the brethren.
The current language of the Code is permissive with regard to
charitable donations. This Resolution would restrict that permissiveness. By
adopting or rejecting this Resolution, Grand Lodge would determine the types of
charitable donations a Lodge can make. However, we find that the policies and
general purposes of the Grand Lodge are consistent with this Resolution.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 02-03
Adds “Applied Masonry” to Mentor Program
The proponents of this Resolution seek to add a required subject to the Grand
Lodge Mentor Program.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
st

WHEREAS, we are now in the 21 Century, Freemasonry should enter
a third stage called “Applied Freemasonry” as it begins to serve the human race,
its true destiny; and
WHEREAS, Masonry has not yet achieved this goal, partly because of
complacent satisfaction in its superiorities and the atmosphere created by rituals
and fraternal friendships. These are beneficial, but Masonry is not a secret
society or a social club. The changes, which will accompany Masonry’s
transition to the third stage when Masonic principles are put into practical and
helpful use, will not hurt the value, which Masons hold so important and
immutable; and
WHEREAS, much of our Masonic secrecy is no longer needed. People
today are not enchanted with secret societies. And there is nothing secret in what
Masons do. The more the general public knows about Masonry the more useful
the Fraternity will become; and
WHEREAS, Masonry must be more universally understood, both inside
and outside as a way of life which has a helpful, practical, supporting purpose;
and
WHEREAS, there are five stages of life in every organism; birth,
growth, use, decay and death. This applies to life groups, to nations and races.
It applies to religions and systems of government. The stage of usefulness
should start during the stage of growth. Masonry is at the end of its growth stage
and it must turn now to the state of usefulness; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

255

WHEREAS, the new era Masonry should inspire all Masons to live a
Masonic life. How a Mason lives outside the Lodge is much more important
than what he does in it. Taking another degree, another oath, learning a new sign
does not necessarily evidence forward steps in Masonic living; and
WHEREAS, we need not change our rituals. What we need is a change
in the understanding of them. We need not change precepts or doctrines or
duties. What we need is to recognize their significance and how to apply them in
practical useful ways in our daily lives; and
WHEREAS, historically Masonry resists change. But humanity is
changing and like it or not Masonry, consisting of human beings, cannot divorce
itself from human life; and
WHEREAS, there are many ways of making “connections” between
Masonry and the community so that the principles and concepts of Masonry can
be “Applied”; and
WHEREAS, “Applied Masonry” will reduce our concern, and rightfully
so, in regard to our public image. It will also give us the answers to the
following questions:
•

What does the community and non-Masons think and know about
us?

•

How are we regarded by the man on the street, both as individuals
and as a group?

•

How can we gain the respect of the non-Mason as long as we are
unable to give a good account or a reason for our existence?

•

Where will he get his inspiration to become a Mason?

•

How can we expect any worthy and honorable man to join our
ranks without any knowledge of what his application will lead him
into; and

WHEREAS, an enlightened and vocal membership active in the
community, as well as the Lodge, will provide the incentive for a favorable,
enhanced and exemplary public image; and
WHEREAS, we can proclaim by our actions that Freemasonry, in its
every effort and purpose, strives to do charitable work within its membership
and for society. Through the teachings it seeks to make good men better men.
We can proudly state that the basic ethical principles as exemplified in our ritual
and Lodge work are such as are acceptable to all good men. All of these lessons
based on the Golden Rule, tolerance toward all mankind, respect for the Family

256

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

and charity toward all, will not be visible in our communities until displayed by
taking THE THIRD STEP, that of “Applied Masonry”.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that this Grand Lodge
adopt the foregoing recommendation as made in the 1989 Spring Issue of the
California Freemason by Brother Ralph H. Head, Editor, to be part of the
curriculum of the Mentor program of the Masonic Education Committee.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Over the past few years, Grand Lodge has made several changes in its
procedures in an effort to make Masonry more relevant and applicable to
Masons and even to society as a whole. We have instituted the Friend to Friend
program to introduce potential candidates and their families to the practical and
meaningful aspects of our activities. We have added to our proficiency exam a
section that requires candidates to display an understanding of the meaning of
our ritual rather than just a rote memorization of its words. Finally, we have
adopted a mentor program to allow candidates to develop an understanding of
our history, symbols, and customs as they progress through their degrees.
It is with regard to this latter program that the authors of Resolution 0203 are concerned. The contents of the mentoring program are heavily oriented to
material that is relevant to activities that either have occurred, or are currently
occurring, inside a Lodge room. It is not readily apparent from this material how
Masonry is to be lived outside of that Lodge room.
Our current society is in a moral decline. Self-interest, greed, and
marketing have replaced brotherly love, relief, and truth. It has been regularly
stated at previous Annual Communications that our society is in need of Masonic
principles and values. As noted in this Resolution, “How a Mason lives outsides
the Lodge is much more important than what he does in it.” But how can we
impact on that society? How can we provide a moral balance to people who
would otherwise not be exposed to our value system?
The proponents of this Resolution believe that one method would be to
emphasize to our newer members the concept of “Applied Masonry”, namely the
practice of Masonry outside of the Lodge room. They would have the mentors
spend some of the educational time emphasizing ways of living Masonic values
on a day-to-day basis and encouraging non-Masons to do the same. While the
Resolution does not provide the specifics of such an educational effort, the
principle is exemplary and is consistent with our policies and general purposes.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

257

Report of the Committee on Finance:
This Resolution No. 02-03, if adopted, will result in approximately
$2,000.00 in cost to the Grand Lodge to republish the current Mentoring
Handbooks.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 02-04
Lodge Youth Award
The proponents of this Resolution seek to create a Lodge Youth Award.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, it is the mission of the California Masonic Youth Orders
Committee to inspire within each Lodge a desire by its members to support and
attend the meetings and functions of our Youth Orders within our jurisdiction;
and
WHEREAS, communication and interaction between Lodges and the
Youth Orders are lacking and are often nonexistent; and
WHEREAS, Lodge Masters often need help in performing this
important duty of their Lodges and a way to encourage and reward this service
and support would be useful.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 15165 be
added to the California Masonic Code as follows:
§15165

WORSHIPFUL MASTER’S LODGE YOUTH SERVICE
AWARD

The Worshipful Master’s Youth Service Award may be presented to
Master Masons in good standing for their exemplary support and
service to one or more of the youth orders of this jurisdiction who
communicate with the Master’s Lodge at Stated Meetings and are
liaisons representing the Lodge to the Youth Orders. The period of
service for this award shall coincide with the Master’s term of office
and the award will be presented at the will and pleasure of the
Worshipful Master. The award shall consist of a lapel pin and a
certificate to be designed by the Youth Orders Committee, with the
approval of the Grand Master. The certificate shall be signed by the
Grand Master, the Grand Secretary, Chairman of the Youth Orders

258

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Committee and the Worshipful Master. The award, by request of the
Lodge Secretary, will be made available through the Grand Lodge’s
supply office, at cost, to the Lodges.
Presentation of the award(s) should be made at a meaningful
celebration event held in conjunction with the appropriate Youth
Group(s) as far as is possible and during the proclaimed Grand Lodge
Youth Orders Month as scheduling permits.
The names of the recipients will be provided to the Chairman of the
Youth Orders Committee for information relative to future nominations
for the Grand Master’s Youth Support Award.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Resolution seeks to create an award that could be given by any
Lodge to one of its members for “exemplary support and service to one or more
of the youth orders of this jurisdiction…” According to the proponents, the
purpose of this Resolution is to help encourage Masons to support youth
activities by rewarding them with a lapel pin and certificate if they do in fact
demonstrate such support.
Promoting and supporting Masonic youth
organizations is, and should be, an important part of every Mason’s life.
However, the prospect of receiving an award and recognition for support of our
youth is not, and should not be, a motivational tool. Masons should support
youth organizations because they believe it is the right thing to do, not because
they might receive an award. As an example, the Hiram Award was not
established to encourage support of a Lodge by its members, but to reward that
support. In any event, each of the youth orders already bestow their own awards
on Masons who are active in their organizations. There is no need for Grand
Lodge to provide additional awards.
This Resolution also provides that the names of the recipients of the
proposed award be forwarded to the Grand Lodge Youth Committee for
potential nomination for the Grand Master’s Youth Support Award. California
Masonic Code Section 15165 already permits such nominations making this
Resolution duplicative of current Masonic law.
This Resolution is not consistent with Grand Lodge policy.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

259

Report of the Committee on Finance:
Resolution No. 02-04 would cost Grand Lodge $3,000.00 in start-up
costs to design and make a pin.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 02-05
Rules for Lodge Board of Trustees
The proponents of this Resolution seek to establish rules for a Lodge Board of
Trustees.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, each Lodge within this jurisdiction is permitted to elect a
Board of Trustees to assist the Lodge in managing and investing the Lodge’s
funds; and
WHEREAS, each Board of Trustees is governed by a Standing
Resolution promulgated by the Grand Lodge as well as relevant provisions of the
California Masonic Code; and
WHEREAS, the Standing Resolution states, in part, “Sec. 8. The
Trustees shall hold the assets of the Lodge and manage the same without
compensation but shall be bonded at the expense of the Lodge”; and
WHEREAS, there is adequate information contained in the California
Masonic Code to assist Lodges, Temple Associations and Life Membership
Committees in the management of assets; and
WHEREAS, the only information and guidance provided to the Board
of Trustees are contained within Section 21030 of the California Masonic Code
and the Standing Resolution; and
WHEREAS, the responsibilities placed on the Board of Trustees may
expose the members of any Board of Trustees to individual liability; and
WHEREAS, it has already been established that By-Laws are beneficial
to the operation of Lodges and Temples Associations (California Masonic Code
Sections 19040 Uniform Code of By-Laws for Lodges and 22010(c) Uniform
Articles of Incorporation and By-Laws for temple Properties); and
WHEREAS, the members of any Board of Trustees should be assisted
in their duties by the establishment of clear cut and unambiguous guidelines.

260

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that in every Lodge within
this jurisdiction that has elected or will elect a Board of Trustees, said Board of
Trustees will be governed in accordance with the Uniform By-Laws for Boards
of Trustees, which are articulated below, in addition to other relevant sections of
the California Masonic Code and the Standing Resolution.
1.

The Trustees shall be responsible for the management of all
assets of the Lodge, including the facilities, properties, and
monies of the Lodge, in accordance with Sections 3 and 8 of
the Standing Resolution. The Board shall directly manage all
fiscal assets of the Lodge not supervised by the Lodge Officers
(specifically those falling under the Lodge Annual Operating
Budget) or assigned by the Lodge to the care, custody and
control of Lodge Associations and Committees, I.E., Lodge
Building Association(s), Life Membership Committee and/or
Special Committees appointed to manage Lodge events,
projects or activities. The California Masonic Code (CMC)
rules, regulations, by-laws and standing resolutions of the
Lodge, as well as applicable State and Federal Law, shall
govern all activities.

2.

The Trustees shall be authorized to invest Lodge assets in
accounts with banks, brokerage firms or mutual fund
companies. The Trustees shall not invest more than seventy
percent (70%) of the Lodge fiscal assets in any single bank,
brokerage or fund company.

3.

The unanimous approval of all Trustees is required for any
transaction involving Lodge assets that has not been previously
authorized by the Trustees in a regular automatic withdrawal
or reinvestment program. The Trustees shall also unanimously
agree to agree to the issuance of any checks or financial
instrument. In the event that a Trustee is not available to
perform their duties due to illness, travel, etc., the Master of
the Lodge may serve in place of the absent Trustee. If less
than a majority of Trustees are available, the prior approval of
the Lodge shall be required. It is implicit that the Lodge shall
replace a Trustee for continued unavailability or other cause.

4.

Account statements provided by a bank, brokerage or mutual
fund company shall be sufficient to record all fiscal activities
within that organization.

5.

The Trustees shall convene a regularly scheduled asset
management review meeting, open to the Lodge membership,
not less often than semi-annually. The Trustees shall share

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

261

equal authority, but shall select one Trustee as Chairman and
shall also appoint a Recorder (who may be a member of the
lodge or another Trustee) for each meeting. Any Lodge
member may attend. Members may actively participate in the
meeting, under Robert’s Rule of Order, at the discretion of the
Chair. Minutes of the meeting shall include a record of any
significant changes in resource allocation or investments
approved by the Trustees between meetings. The Minutes
shall be provided to the Secretary of the Lodge for
presentation at the following Stated Meeting of the Lodge.
Acceptance of the Minutes of the trustee Management Review
meetings by the membership shall signify approval of the
Trustee’s actions not previously recorded by the Lodge.
6.

The Trustees shall be authorized to engage the services of a
Certified Public Accountant and/or tax consultant, at Lodge
expense, to assist the Board regarding any accounting and/or
tax issues that should arise.

7.

Any amendments to these By-Laws will be made by the Grand
Lodge Committee on Investments, with approval of the Grand
Master, and shall be adopted for use by each Board of
Trustees. Recommendations for amendments or revisions to
these Uniform By-Laws for Boards of Trustees may be
submitted to the Committee on Investments by any Master
Mason in Good Standing within this jurisdiction.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to establish rules for the
governance of Boards of Trustees elected by a Lodge and to adopt uniform bylaws for the aforesaid Boards of Trustees in addition to provisions presently
contained in California Masonic Code Section 21030 and the Standing
Resolution.
In order to ensure proper investment and administrative control and
surveillance of Lodge assets, the Grand Lodge of California has provided for the
creation of an investment committee appointed by the Master of the Lodge to
serve as a lodge committee at the pleasure of the Worshipful Master or
alternatively for Boards of Trustees of the Lodge to be elected by the Lodges
and to serve for specific staggered terms. The latter system has proven to be
adequate for the purposes stated in most cases. Exceptions generally fall into the
following categories: 1) mismanagement by reason of inattentiveness of the
trustees; 2) permitting one or more of the trustees to control and administer the
assets to the exclusion of the others; 3) failure to make the necessary decisions
and investments; 4) to expend monies without due investigation or

262

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

consideration; or in rare cases, 5) permitting one trustee not only to be the
“boss” but to invest or expend funds without proper backup or advice and by
independent execution of checks covering disbursements or worse. The trustees
in such cases abdicate their authority and responsibility in favor of one or
another officer or officers of the lodge, a fellow trustee or trustees.
This Resolution appears to be for administrative purposes and for the
proper control and investment of Lodge assets. It does not violate the policy or
purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Grand Lodge Temple Properties and Taxation Committee to the Masonic

Resolution No. 02-07
Requires Temple Associations for Real Property
The proponents of this Resolution seek to clarify the California Masonic Code to
require that Lodges use an incorporated Temple Association for the purpose of
purchasing, erecting or maintaining and holding title to real estate for a temple
for Lodge uses and purposes.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, ownership of real estate and buildings exposes the owners
and Lodge members to certain liability risks; and
WHEREAS, corporation status, if properly constructed, can shield
Lodges and their members from such liability risk, such as personal injury and
financial risk due to loan forfeiture; and
WHEREAS, Grand Lodge has over the last few years created Uniform
By-Laws to be used for the incorporation of Temple Associations, whether the
Temple is owned by one or more Lodges jointly; and
WHEREAS, the various sections of the CMC which deal with
ownership and maintenance of Lodge assets seem to permit management of
Lodge assets either by Trustees (see Sections 21010 and 21040) or by
incorporated Temple Associations (see Section 22010); and
WHEREAS, the Trustee arrangement can expose Lodges and their
members to the aforesaid risks, which can be minimized or eliminated by
corporation status.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the California Masonic
Code be amended as follows:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

§ 22010. ORGANIZATION AND OPERATION.
All Lodges shall use an incorporated Temple Association for the
purpose of purchasing, erecting or maintaining and holding title to real
estate for a temple for Lodge uses and purposes, and a Lodge or Lodges
may hold shares of stock in such corporation. Lodges shall seek and
receive the consent of the Grand Lodge Committee on Temple
Properties and Taxation before establishing such corporation, and may
reduce the annual per capita payment to Grand Lodge by the amount of
the California corporation filing fee(s) incurred in establishing and
maintaining such corporation. Lodges with such existing corporations
shall seek review by the Grand Lodge Committee on Temple Properties
and Taxation of its corporate status and the extent to which that status
shields Lodge members from liability, to the extent permissible by
California State law.
All Temple Associations shall be organized and shall operate as
follows:
A.

A Temple Association may be organized either as a non-profit
or as a general law corporation under the laws of the State in
which it owns or is to acquire real estate, land or a building;

B.

The exclusive purpose for which a Temple Association may be
organized is to hold title to property, collect income therefrom
and turn over the entire amount thereof, less expenses, to its
members or shareholders, all of which must be organizations
described in Section 501(c) of the Internal Revenue Code of
1954. A Temple Association shall not engage in any activities
or exercise any powers that are not in furtherance of this
exclusive purpose. No part of the net earnings of a Temple
Association shall inure to the benefit of any member or private
shareholder, as defined for purposes of Section 501(c)(7) of
the Internal Revenue Code of 1954;

C.

Uniform Articles of Incorporation and By-Laws for Temple
Associations written by the Grand Lodge Committee on
Temple Properties, with the approval of the Grand Master,
shall be adopted for use by each Temple Association. Any
amendments to such Uniform Articles or By-laws by the
Grand Lodge Committee on Temple Properties, with the
approval of the Grand Master, also shall be adopted for use by
each Temple Association. Appropriate revisions to the
Uniform Articles and By-laws may be authorized by the
Committee on Temple Properties for Temple Associations
with more than one member or shareholder;

265

266

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

D.

A Lodge shall exercise its rights as a member or shareholder of
a Temple Association, including its right to elect or remove
directors, pursuant only to the authorization of a majority of
members of the Lodge present at a Stated Meeting of the
Lodge at which a quorum is present. Such authorization shall
not be contested if written instruments, executed by the Master
or Secretary of the Lodge, setting forth the authorization are
filed with the Secretary of the Temple Association. The Lodge
may exercise its rights through the Master of the Lodge, or any
person authorized by proxy or power of attorney duly executed
by him who is authorized to execute any documents and to
vote the Lodge’s membership or shares at any members’ or
shareholders’ meeting of the Temple Association in
accordance with the terms of any action previously authorized
by the Lodge;

E.

A Temple Association shall submit a written report to each
Lodge which is a member or shareholder of the Association on
August 1 of each year and on the following February 1. The
report shall be presented at the next Stated Meeting of the
Lodge. The August 1 report shall include a statement of
income and expenditures for the six month period ending on
the preceding June 30, together with a statement of the assets
and liabilities of the Association on the preceding June 30.
The February 1 report shall include a statement of income and
expenditures for the twelve month period ending on the
preceding December 31, together with a statement of the assets
and liabilities of the Association on the preceding December
31. A copy of each such report shall be given to the Inspector
of the District to which such Lodge is assigned;

F.

Annually on or before the 15th day of the fifth month
following the end of its fiscal year, each Temple Association
shall submit to the Grand Secretary a full and correct report of
its transactions for that preceding fiscal year on a form as may
be provided. The report shall include a statement of assets,
liabilities and net worth as of the end of the 12 month period.
Temple Associations formed during the 12 month period shall
transmit a report from the date of incorporation;

G.

A Temple Association may not hold or administer funds for
any purpose not specifically set forth in this Code; and

H.

Temple Associations funds, not Lodge funds, must be used to
compensate employees.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

267

This Resolution would require that all Lodges use an incorporated
Temple Association for the purpose of purchasing, erecting, maintaining or
holding title to real estate for a Lodge Temple. This Resolution would also
reduce the annual per capita payment to Grand Lodge by the amount of the
California Corporation filing fees incurred in establishing and maintaining said
corporation. It would also require existing Temple Associations to seek review
of their corporate status by said Grand Lodge Committee.
The proposed new verbiage would be added to the existing California
Masonic Code Section 22010.
Presently, Sections 21010, 21040 and 21060 are permissive and use the
word “may” in reference to their provisions. The proposed amendment uses the
mandatory word “shall” to require that all Lodges use an incorporated Temple
Association for the purpose as stated.
By the use of the incorporated Temple Associations, Lodges and their
members may be shielded from liability for personal injuries and from financial
risks due to loan foreclosure. The Resolution would also clarify Sections 21010,
21040 and 21060 and make them applicable only to property other than Lodge
temples. This Resolution is distinguished from Grand Master Whitaker’s
Recommendation No. 1 which seeks to have all real estate of a Lodge held by a
corporation.
It has been the policy of the Grand Lodge for many years to require all
Lodges holding title to their Lodge Hall to become incorporated as a Temple
Association.
Although incorporation should not be considered a substitute for
adequate insurance, the proposed Resolution serves the best interests and general
purposes of our Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
Resolution No. 02-07 would permit a lodge to reduce the per capita
assessment paid to Grand Lodge by the amount of the initial filing fees for
incorporation and the annual filing fee. There are approximately 250 Hall
Associations currently with an annual filing fee of $10.00 each. The initial filing
fees for new Hall Associations could be as high as several thousand dollars each.

268

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Should this Resolution pass, the minimum annual cost to Grand Lodge
would be $2,500.00 plus the cost of any new associations.
Resolution CARRIED-OVER.

Resolution No. 02-08
Procedures for Tax-Exempt Donations to Temple Associations
The proponents of this Resolution seek to modify the California Masonic Code
to explicitly require that the Grand Lodge Temple Properties and Taxation
Committee develop and maintain procedures whereby Lodges may effectively
use their non-profit status to solicit tax-deductible donations.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Lodges are typically established as non-profit entities
under IRS Code Section 501(c)(10); and
WHEREAS, 501(c)(10) status allows solicitation of tax exempt
donations, exactly like Section 501(c)(3); and
WHEREAS, tax exempt status applies to donations submitted for the
following purposes, “Religious, Educational, Charitable, Scientific, Literary,
Testing for Public Safety, to Foster National or International Amateur Sports
Competition, or Prevention of Cruelty to Children or Animals Organizations”;
and
WHEREAS, IRS Revenue Ruling 75-470 specifies that preservation of
historical buildings qualifies as a “charitable” activity: “Preservation of
buildings having historical or architectural significance – A non-profit
organization formed to promote an appreciation of history through the
acquisition, restoration, and preservation of homes, churches, and public
buildings having special historical or architectural significance and to open the
structures for viewing by the general public qualifies for exemption under IRC
501(c)(3)”; and
WHEREAS, all Lodges can be benefited by access to general
information as to how to solicit tax exempt donations, what purposes are
appropriate for such solicitation, what notification must be given to donors, and
what risks (if any) are entailed in such use of non-profit status.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 6050 of the
California Masonic Code be amended to read:

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

269

§ 6050. TEMPLE PROPERTIES AND TAXATION.
The Committee on Temple Properties shall examine and approve all
details regarding any contract involving a Lodge, Temple Association,
committee, partnership or corporation or other entity representing itself
to be Masonic in character, or operating for the benefit of a Lodge or
Lodges in this Jurisdiction for:
A.

Any option or contract for the purchase of real property, the
encumbrance of real property or the lease of real property, as
lessor or lessee, for more than three years;

B.

A contract for or commencement of the construction,
alteration, improvement, replacement, repair, or maintenance
of a Masonic Temple in excess of $15,000; and

C.

Any option or contract regarding the sale or listing for sale of
any real property owned by such entity.

The committee shall, subject to the approval of the Grand Master,
establish rules and regulations governing the processing of applications
for committee approval.
The committee shall review the reports of transactions of Temple
Associations submitted to the Grand Secretary as provided for
elsewhere in this Code and may give counsel and advise concerning the
operation of those Temple Associations.
The committee shall, also, advise Lodges and the Grand Lodge on
matters of taxation with respect to income, property, sales and
employment taxes. In so doing, the Committee shall develop and
maintain complete procedures whereby Lodges and Temple
Associations may potentially utilize their non-profit status to solicit tax
deductible donations for maintaining Temple properties, and other
appropriate and permissible purposes.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Resolution would require the Grand Lodge Committee on Temple
Properties to develop procedures to assist Lodges and Temple Associations in
soliciting charitable donations. The law on such donations is extremely
complex. This Resolution would put Grand Lodge in the position of giving tax
advice. Grand Lodge does not and should not give tax advice to anyone. Doing
so could subject it to liability. The procedure a Lodge must follow to properly
solicit charitable donations should be done on the advice of tax professionals.
The cost of obtaining such tax advice should be borne by the Lodge seeking it.

270

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

This Resolution is not consistent with Grand Lodge policy or general
purposes.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
This Resolution No. 02-08, if passed, will result in approximately
$500.00 in cost to the Grand Lodge to reprint the Temple Association
Handbook.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 02-09
Partnerships with Schools
The proponents of this Resolution seek to permit Lodges to establish
partnerships with local schools.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the Grand Lodge of California has requested that the
Lodges of California participate with the Public Schools Program in their areas;
and
WHEREAS, most Lodges have a Public Schools Program; and
WHEREAS, the Public Schools Program is a valuable tool for the
community and the Lodges of California.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 27600 of the
California Masonic Code be amended to read as follows:
§27600.

ADDITIONAL FINANCIAL SUPPORT FOR MASONIC
LODGES.

A Masonic Lodge may solicit and receive financial support for its
fraternal operations and charitable activities from the following
additional sources:
A.

A Masonic Lodge may hold periodic activities to which tickets
are sold to its members and their families and friends. No
tickets to such an activity may be sold to the general public nor
may there be any other public solicitation of support for such
an activity. The sponsor shall disclose that any profits from
the activity will be used for the sponsor’s fraternal and not

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

charitable purposes, if such is the case. In the event that such
disclosure is not given, through inadvertence or otherwise, all
proceeds from the activity shall be given to a recognized
Masonic charity.
B.

Advertising may be included in publications which the Lodge
uses to communicate with its members, provided that:
1.

The advertisements are in good taste and within the
bounds of propriety;

2.

Advertisements for controlled substances, alcohol, or
tobacco are not accepted;

3.

Advertisements for religious or political groups are not
accepted;

4.

Lodge mailing lists or Grand Lodge rosters are not
furnished to advertisers;

5.

No representation is made to Masons that it is their duty to
advertise;

6.

Advertising is accepted only from members and
businesses or commercial establishments owned or
operated by members of the Lodge. In joint publications
to the members of the Lodge and to the members of one or
more other Lodges or Masonic Organizations, advertising
is accepted only from members and businesses or
commercial establishments owned or operated by
members of one or more of the participating Lodges and
Masonic Organizations;

7.

Solicitation of advertisements is by a member in good
standing of a Lodge or Masonic Organization for whose
publication advertising is solicited; and

8.

Any advertising revenue in excess of the expenses of
publishing, printing, and mailing such publication is
donated to a recognized Masonic charity.

C.

A Masonic Lodge may participate in local community fairs,
celebrations and similar community events by sponsoring,
occupying and operating booths for selling food or nonalcoholic beverages to persons attending or participating in
such events.

D.

A Masonic Lodge may participate in any other activity which
has been approved by the Grand Master.

271

272

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

E.

A Lodge may establish partnerships with the Public Schools
within its area to conduct fund raising activities whereby both
the Lodge and the Public Schools will benefit from the
proceeds of the fund raising activity.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of Resolution No. 02-09 seek to establish a partnership
between local Lodges and schools to conduct fund raising activities by adding
paragraph “C” to Section 27600 of the California Masonic Code. In essence,
Paragraph “C” would permit a Lodge to split the proceeds from any jointly
operated fund raising activity. This would be a violation of Paragraph A of
Section 27600 which prohibits public solicitation to support a Lodge.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy and general purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Resolution WITHDRAWN.

Resolution No. 02-10
Declared Out Of Order By The Grand Master
Resolution No. 02-11
Listing Prince Hall Grand Lodges in List of Lodges Masonic
The proponents of this resolution seek to correct the omission of Prince Hall
Grand Lodges from the List of Lodges – Masonic.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, the Regularity of Prince Hall Masonry is now widely
accepted, and “. . . the philosophy and practice of Prince Hall Masonry today are
of exemplary regularity,” (Brother Paul M. Bessel, Masonic Leadership Center
Website, “Prince Hall Masonry Recognition Details”); and
WHEREAS, two-thirds of the Grand Lodges in the United States “have
adopted resolutions that say Prince Hall Masonry is Regular” (Paul M. Bessel,
Masonic Leadership Center Website, “Prince Hall Masonry Recognition
Details”), 27 have extended full recognition, and others have extended
recognition or recognized Regularity and are apparently in various stages of
negotiation with Prince Hall Masonry; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

273

WHEREAS, 8 of 10 Grand Lodges have jurisdiction in the Provinces of
Canada, the United Grand Lodge of England, the United Grand Lodges of
Germany, the Grand Lodges of France, Ireland Scotland, Netherlands (Grand
Orient), Belgium (Regular Grand Lodge), Austria, Philippines, Japan, New
South Wales & ACT, Queensland, South Australia & Northern Territory,
Tasmania, Victoria, Western Australia, and New Zealand have all extended
recognition to some or all of the Most Worshipful Prince Hall Grand Lodges;
and
WHEREAS, the failure to recognize the Regularity of Prince Hall
Masonry is an extraordinary situation with its roots in the past slavery and
segregation that existed in the United States and has nothing to do with the actual
Regularity of these Lodges; and
WHEREAS, it is high time that this Most Worshipful Grand Lodge and,
indeed, all Masonic Grand Lodges remove every aspect of “The Ugly Blemish of
Freemasonry” (Norman Bargo, The Philalethes, August 2001).
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that because of the
extraordinary circumstances surrounding the failure to recognize the Regularity
of Prince Hall Masonry, The List of Lodges – Masonic should henceforth do
their part in rectifying the grievous errors of past and present and include the 41
Most Worshipful Prince Hall Grand Lodges in the 2003 issue of their directory
and subsequent directories.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that our Most Worshipful Grand
Master contact the publishers and make this resolution of our Most Worshipful
Grand Lodge known to them.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that if the publishers of The List of
Lodges – Masonic do not agree to begin listing Prince Hall Grand Lodges in the
2003 issue of their directory, our Very Worshipful Grand Secretary is directed to
present a Resolution to the next Conference of Masonic Grand Lodge Secretaries
in North America calling on them to make a similar demand of the publishers of
The List of Lodges – Masonic.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that if the Conference of Masonic
Grand Lodge Secretaries does not accept such Resolution and/or the publishers
of The List of Lodges – Masonic continue to refuse to include Prince Hall Grand
Lodges as Regular Masonic Grand Lodges in their next publication, this Most
Worshipful Grand Lodge is directed to enlist the cooperation of the 34 United
States Grand Lodges who now extend recognition to Prince Hall Masonry in
seeking a new publisher to publish a directory of Recognized Masonic Grand
Lodges that does include Prince Hall Masonry.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this Most Worshipful Grand
Lodge obtain, or compile, and distribute to our Lodges at least biannually a list
of Prince Hall Lodges in California and a list of Prince Hall Grand Lodges. The

274

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

list should include the names and addresses of California Prince Hall Lodge
Secretaries and Grand Lodge Grand Secretaries.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Resolution No. 02-11 is a Resolution that should be discussed and
should be debated, not for what it seeks to do but for what has to be done. The
authors of the Resolution are correct, 36 Lodges out of 51 in the United States
have, in some form, recognized Prince Hall. All but one are in the Northern,
Eastern and Western portions of our country. While one Grand Lodge in the Old
Confederacy has established recognition at least to a degree, one Lodge in the
Northeastern part of our nation has not. Gradually, recognition is being obtained
on a mutual basis.
You cannot tell the publisher of a book that he must do something.
This is our American way of free enterprise. The publisher of List of Lodges
Masonic is in business. He must of necessity cater to his normal audience if he
is to survive. What the proponents of Resolution 02-11 seek to enforce is
something that is emotionally commendable but practically and legally it is not.
This Committee is aware that efforts are being made to do the very thing
voluntarily that the proponents of this Resolution wish to do by mandate. We
are working together with the List of Lodges Masonic and with the Prince Hall
Masonic Directory, which by the way is much more detailed in California than
the List of Lodges Masonic. It took a long while for recognition to be effective
in this State by the two Grand Lodges. It was painstaking but cooperative. We
are reaching a point where each Mason is recognized as a full and favorable
person. This Resolution requires the Grand Master to act in a way in which he is
not empowered. It requires the Grand Secretary to act in a way in which he is
not empowered. Neither has any extra-territorial powers. It behooves us not to
try to dictate but rather to reason. We accomplish more that way. While this
Grand Lodge can do anything it wishes, the Committee on Policy and General
Purposes believes this is not the way to accomplish the stated result.
This Resolution is not consistent with our present policy and general
purposes.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
The issue for this Resolution is to determine which of the Prince Hall
Grand Lodges and Lodges are recognized by the Grand Lodge of California. It
is estimated that the cost to accumulate and research the Prince Hall Grand
Lodges and Lodges would be $24,000.00.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

would extend financial aid to more students. One of our primary goals as

This Resolution is consistent with the policy and general purposes of
